Nothing else changed: the first run still starts as soon as step 1 is saved, and the page still asks the server for progress while you answer. A test fails if any of steps 1 to 4, the strip or their live text contains scan, discover, queued, found or Sentinel (the word scanner is allowed only on the plan step's option cards).
- **The server image installs the newest Sentinel `main` at build time.** `web/requirements.txt` keeps the commit pin as the known-good floor (CI and the tests use it, and three CI checks read it); the Dockerfile's `SENTINEL_REF` build argument (default `main`) is resolved to a commit when the image is built (`scripts/sentinel-ref.sh`, passed as `SENTINEL_SHA`) and installed over it, in `make up`, `make build`, `scripts/update.sh` and the approved update. The image labels `com.cvengine.sentinel.ref` and `.sha` record the request. Why not `@main`: [docs/UPDATES.md](docs/UPDATES.md#the-sentinel-library-in-the-server-image).
- **A Sentinel that follows the instance default is Automatic while Keep Sentinels on the newest version is on** (it used to follow the instance's own Automatic or Manual update choice). Turning the switch off, or choosing a value for the instance or one Sentinel, restores the old behaviour.
- **The setup wizard's last screen says only that things are getting ready, and shows only while no host has been found.** It no longer reads "Building your workspace" with a checklist (Sentinel ready, Discovery running, Hosts found), a "Latest:" list of host names and "The first discovery is running": that gave away that a scan had already begun. Now: "Getting everything ready", "This takes about a minute.", a calm bar with no percentage, and a closing "Opening your dashboard". With one or more hosts already found when someone reaches or finishes the step (or opens `/cve/welcome/` there), the dashboard opens at once with no screen in between. With none, the screen stays at most `CVENGINE_SETUP_DWELL_SECONDS` (default now 60, was 90; `0` turns it off), counted from the first time the screen asked so a reload does not restart it, and leaves the moment the first host appears. The two engineers keep their banter, minus the lines that mention scanning.
- **The built-in scanner follows the newest published Sentinel instead of a digest a person pins** ([docs/BUILTIN-SCANNER.md](docs/BUILTIN-SCANNER.md#keeping-it-current-the-scanner-update)). The `scanner` service runs `ghcr.io/hevnsnt/cvengine-sentinel:main`, the tag the Sentinel repository moves at every build of its `main` branch (which only receives code after its checks pass; images carry an SBOM and build provenance), so a Sentinel fix reaches a fresh install, and an installed one at its next update or scheduled run, with nobody re-pinning. `scanner.lock.json` is now the known-good floor the release was tested with (commit, contract version, an optional digest kept for provenance and for `make scanner-build`), and `scripts/scanner_lock.py` and CI check that the compose default is the moving tag, that the floor commit exists on the Sentinel's `main` and that its contract version is not above what the server reads. A new *Scanner update* (`scripts/scanner-update.sh`, `make scanner-update`, about daily from the updater timer, one cron line otherwise) pulls the tag, lets the scanner finish its tasks, starts the new container and keeps it only once it is healthy and has checked in with a contract version the server accepts; otherwise the previous image comes back and the Sentinels page shows why. `CVENGINE_SCANNER_IMAGE` (a digest or a tag) holds a version and is never pulled over or rolled back. The Sentinels page and System status show the running commit, whether it is the newest the server saw, and since when; a scanner older than the server speaks says *This scanner is older than this server needs, update it* and is offered no work.

- **Settings, AI model says which model does which job, and every model is a drop-down.** The page opens with one **Connection** block (provider, API key, address, and a status line: "Connected: 13 models available", or "Could not reach Anthropic:" and the plain reason; one **Test connection**), then a table **Which model does which job** with three rows: Everything else (the default model: triage, CVE confirmation, Nuclei planning), Finding review and Operations check (the AI reviewer's two models, moved here from the reviewer card). Each row is a drop-down filled from the provider's live list, fetched when the page opens and when the provider, key or address changes (debounced, stale requests aborted, kept in memory per provider and address, **Refresh list**); it shows the price per million input tokens where the provider reports it, marks the recommended model, always keeps the saved model as an option, and offers **Other (type a model name)...** for your own servers, gateways and a list that could not load. The old **Load models** button and the free-text boxes are gone, and the setup wizard and the Connect-a-model page use the same drop-down. One **Save AI settings** saves everything and says what is in force. `POST /cve/api/settings/ai/models` uses the saved key when none is typed (never returned or logged), caches the list for five minutes and a failure for thirty seconds in Redis keyed by a hash of the key, returns at most 500 models, and answers a failure in plain words. The reviewer API is unchanged. No migration.
- **A simpler shell: the left rail goes from 15 links to 6.** Overview, Findings, Assets, Scans, Scanners and Bug bounty (only for people authorised for it); Settings, Administration and Help sit in the footer, each only for the roles that can open them. Pages that used to have their own entry are tabs inside the one they belong to: Findings (All findings, CVE matches, Config checks, Changes, CVE catalog), Assets (Hosts, Targets, Graph, Secrets, Third parties), Scans (Scans, Activity), Scanners (Health, Sentinels, This project, Integrations). Every existing address still opens the same page, and the Ctrl K palette still finds all of them. The phone tab bar is Overview, Findings, Assets, Scans, More, Account, and More lists what the bar and rail do not.
- **One Scanners page** shows scanner health (workers, scheduler, queues) without a Refresh click; Sentinels and API tokens and MCP are tabs of it.
- **Settings is Essentials (AI model, Alerts, Reports, Updates) plus a collapsed Advanced group.** Notifications and Email are one Alerts pane; OpenVAS settings live only in Administration. Labels: Live is Overview, Hygiene is Config checks, Suppliers is Third parties, About is Help.
- **The dashboard asks for its trend charts only when they are about to be seen**, instead of loading up to 1,000 findings and 1,000 changes on every visit, and the shell no longer repeats its scans request.
- **The PDF renderer runs at a lower CPU priority** (`nice -n 10` on the report child process; skipped where `nice` is missing), so laying out a long report does not starve the web server and the database sharing the host.

### Fixed

- **Load fixes from the second fresh install.** The built-in scanner no longer runs itself out of memory: it weighs every kind of work against its own container limit and starts only what fits, a killed tool reports *out of memory, retry later*, and `SCANNER_MEM_LIMIT` defaults to 3g with the measured peaks in [docs/DEPLOYMENT.md](docs/DEPLOYMENT.md#memory). The claim limiter grows with a Sentinel's slots (`max(60, 30 x max_concurrent)` calls a minute) and a 429 carries `Retry-After`. The setup wizard holds the first discovery until a scanner is live (no failed scan on a clean install) and keeps its scanner line current; the admin banner prints the moved HTTPS port; `scripts/uninstall-updater.sh` (`make uninstall-updater`) removes the cron line `install.sh` adds; a second checkout on the host no longer shares the first one's Compose project, and the scanner container has no fixed name; with no AI model connected a finding costs one activity line, not four.
- **Screenshots on a host whose AppArmor refuses user namespaces say why** and stop taking `web_screenshot` work, and `config/apparmor/cvengine-scanner` with `docker-compose.apparmor.yml` is the fix ([docs/DEPLOYMENT.md](docs/DEPLOYMENT.md#screenshots-and-apparmor)). A kind the broker withholds after three failures in a row shows as *Paused after repeated failures* with its retry time and last error, not as *Scanner offline*.
- **The per-CVE nuclei checks run again.** Since the AI reviewer phase 2 change `run_cve_tests` takes `ai`, not a provider, but the targeted pass still called it with `provider=`: every check raised `TypeError`, was caught as one CVE failing and recorded `failed` in the service ledger, so a CVE with a matching template was never tested. `run_cve_checks` now takes `ai` (a CVE without a safe template may queue a reviewer-container draft) and no provider object travels to it. A `failed` ledger row is not coverage (`ledger.fresh` counts only `done`), so those CVEs are checked at the next pass with no data fix. A test binds every call site to the real signature.

- Non-admin users no longer see a "Connect your AI model" button that answered 403.
- The Sentinel Provision form starts on the current project, not the first one in the list.
- The dashboard Exposed hosts tile opens the list of exactly those hosts (`hosts/?exposed=1`).

### Changed

- **The server no longer runs nmap or naabu.** The port and service stage, the UDP sweep, the full-port sweep and its follow-up fingerprint, the version probe and the path probe run on a Sentinel only. With no scanner able to take the work the stage waits and the addresses stay due (`ScannerUnavailable`); `CVENGINE_LOCAL_FALLBACK` no longer applies to `nmap`, `naabu`, `nmap_udp`, `nmap_full`, `nmap_verify` or `path_probe`. The server keeps what it does with a report: it reads the Sentinel's XML with the scanner library's parser and stores the ports and services (`cveFeed/port_inventory.py`, `cveFeed/port_stage.py`). `cveFeed/nmap_scan.py` and `cveFeed/naabu_scan.py` are gone.
- **A browser key no longer scores like a password.** A host whose secrets the inventory knows is scored by its worst counting secret: a server secret 25, a browser key 5 (it was 25 for any credential pattern). The score line reads "Server credential served on /" or "Browser key served on /".

- **Seven more scan tools run on the scanner** (Sentinel contract version 3, [docs/SCANNER-ARCHITECTURE.md](docs/SCANNER-ARCHITECTURE.md#b-tool-work-that-became-sentinel-kinds-in-contract-version-3)). The background full port sweep (`nmap_full`), the nmap service probe (`nmap_verify`), the node2shell check, the RDP screenshot (`rdp_shot`), the database exposure check (`db_probe`), the scan path check (`path_probe`) and the AI template check (`nuclei_validate`) are now Sentinel kinds, and the wafw00f stage dispatches the `waf` kind it already had. Each stage asks a scanner first and runs here only when `CVENGINE_LOCAL_FALLBACK` allows it; otherwise it waits and its targets stay due. The path check and the template check go only to the built-in scanner. A Sentinel asks for the new kinds only from a server that has them, so 1.3 servers are unaffected.

### Changed

- **One copy of the scanner code.** The server now imports the scanner library (`cvengine_scanner`, from the Sentinel repository, pinned in
  `web/requirements.txt`) for the web fingerprint readers (technologies, CMS versions, version headers, soft-404 detection), the nmap
  parser, and the scan flags and policy constants (nuclei's `-etags dos`, the reviewed NSE scripts, the UDP port list, the probe ports). The
  server's own copies are gone, so a Sentinel and the server scan and read a host the same way. Nothing changes in what is run, found or stored.
- **More AI work goes through the AI reviewer container** ([docs/AI-REVIEWER.md](docs/AI-REVIEWER.md#the-pipelines-ai-jobs)): detection records and CVE guidance, the auto-tuner's advisor, the RDP login-screen reading (the screenshot travels in the job) and nuclei template drafts are queued as AI jobs and applied when the answer lands, with the same validation, budgets and gates as before. `update_cve_detection` with `guidance` now answers at once with `revision: {state: queued}`; the record changes when the revision lands. The auto-tuner applies its advisor's answer in the round after it was asked, judged against the range in force then.
- **Web screenshots are taken by a Sentinel, not by the server** (contract version 3, kind `web_screenshot`). The server picks the pages and computes the scope policy, and dispatches tasks of at most six pages; the Sentinel runs Playwright's headless Chromium inside its own sandbox (proved before any page opens; a Sentinel that cannot prove it takes no picture and says why), every connection through a vetting proxy that applies the task's scope policy and the Sentinel's address floor, and returns the PNGs. The server checks each picture (PNG signature, header checksum, sides, size, closing chunk) before storing it and never uses a name a Sentinel reports. The kind is `NEVER_LOCAL`: with no Sentinel able to take it the pages stay queued and the button shows "Waiting for a scanner". The server image no longer contains Chromium (about 300 MB smaller) and the `celery` container no longer carries Playwright's seccomp profile: it moves to the `scanner` service and to the Sentinel repository's `sentinel` service. Removed: `cveFeed/shot_worker.py`, `cveFeed/shot_proxy.py`, `manage.py check_screenshot_sandbox`, the `playwright` pin and `CVENGINE_SCREENSHOT_SANDBOX` / `CVENGINE_SCREENSHOT_USER`. A Sentinel needs the matching image to take screenshots; an older one simply never declares the kind.

### Removed

- **1.4.0 carries no edition name.** The `web/.edition` file that put "camstork edition" beside the version on the About page is gone, so the page and the release read "1.4.0" and nothing else. Older release notes keep their history. The optional edition mechanism itself is unchanged.
- **The server's own OpenVAS path is gone.** This server no longer opens a GMP session to a gvmd or runs a Greenbone scan (`openvas` is in `NEVER_LOCAL`, so no fallback setting brings it back): each due batch goes to a Sentinel that runs its own GVM stack, the old in-stack Greenbone containers are unused, and `make openvas-user`, the `openvas_bootstrap`, `openvas_credentials` and `openvas_task_report` commands and the settings test button are removed.
- **Discovery and the RDP screenshot no longer run on the server.** The `bbot_discovery` and `rdp_shot` kinds are `NEVER_LOCAL` (like `secrets_scan`): whatever `CVENGINE_LOCAL_FALLBACK` says, with no Sentinel able to take them the scan waits and the target stays due. The server's own discovery runner and RDP client are deleted.
- **The TLS audit and the WAF fingerprint no longer run on the server.** The two kinds (`testssl` and `waf`) join `secrets_scan` in `sentinel_dispatch.NEVER_LOCAL`: whatever `CVENGINE_LOCAL_FALLBACK` says, they run on a Sentinel only, and with no scanner able to take the work the sweep or the stage waits and the targets stay due. The local runners, their proxy arguments, the port 25 whole-run wait and the `CVENGINE_TESTSSL_TIMEOUT` and `CVENGINE_WAFW00F_TIMEOUT` settings are gone; `cveFeed/testssl_scan.py` and `cveFeed/waf_scan.py` became `cveFeed/tls_audit.py` and `cveFeed/waf_fingerprint.py`, which only choose the targets and store what a Sentinel reports.
- **The three-hourly AI assessment review is retired.** The AI reviewer's operations check ([docs/AI-REVIEWER.md](docs/AI-REVIEWER.md#operations-check-in-detail)) replaces it, and the deterministic coverage top-up and the auto-tuner already did the rest of its work. Migration `cveFeed.0148` removes its schedule and its two tables (`AssessmentReview`, `AssessmentReviewDecision`); the capacity report it kept (`performance_review.review`) and the Scheduler health panel that showed both go with it. The coverage query it shared moves to `cveFeed.coverage_topup`.
- **The server no longer runs nuclei** (`nuclei_light`, `nuclei_targeted`, `nuclei_deep` and `nuclei_validate` join `secrets_scan` in `sentinel_dispatch.NEVER_LOCAL`). The local runners are gone (`stream_nuclei`, the template test and syntax check of `templategen`, the loopback fixture harness, the maintainer's fixture approval of a draft, the signed-in template check): every template pass, template test and AI-template check runs on a scanner, and with none live the stage waits and its targets stay due, whatever `CVENGINE_LOCAL_FALLBACK` says. A signed-in pass runs only on the built-in scanner. The server keeps the static safety gate, the approved template snapshot a scanner downloads, the AI template flow up to a draft, and storing what a scanner reports. A reviewed draft can no longer be promoted on the server (`manage.py review_generated_template` and `web/cveFeed/reviewed_fixtures/` are removed), the CI loopback fixture smoke test is removed, and the one-time re-run of validation checks keyed on the old command line is expected.
- **The web probes no longer run on the server.** `http_probe`, `route_probe`, `vhost_probe`, `subdir_probe` and `supplier_check` join `sentinel_dispatch.NEVER_LOCAL`: whatever `CVENGINE_LOCAL_FALLBACK` says, the version probes, the route check, the virtual host and subdirectory sweeps and the supplier checks are a Sentinel's requests, and with none able to take the work they stay due. The server keeps the rules, the storing and the scheduling; the slot reservation of `slow_web` and the dead local sign-in code (`AuthScope.headers_for`, `accepts_untrusted_certificate`) are gone. (The local request code itself was not gone yet when this entry was first written: the WordPress detection still reached it through `probes.run_http_probe`. It goes with the entry below.)
- **The WordPress detection is a Sentinel's request too, and the last local request code is gone.** `wpscan_scan.detect` (probe `wordpress-detect`: the front page, `wp-login.php`, `wp-json/`, the readme and the upgrade directory, three or more GETs per host) ran from this server through `probes.run_http_probe`. It is now a planned `http_probe` task like every other version probe. With no scanner able to take it nothing is recorded: the sites stay due, none is read as not WordPress, the sites that already show a sign of WordPress are still scanned, and the run says `probe_waiting`. With that, `route_auth.probe_url`, `probes.fetch`, `probes.run_http_probe` and the reading code behind them, the connection classes, `open_socket`, `read_body` and the proxy tunnel of `pinned_transport` and `egress_proxy` are deleted: this server opens no connection to a target, and `pinned_transport.resolve` stays only to refuse a prohibited address before a task is built. The tests that read a page with the removed code now run the scanner library's own (`tests/probe_scanner.py`, `tests/library_http.py`).
- **The server image no longer carries the scan tools.** nmap, naabu, bbot (with its virtual environment, `tzdata` pin and the build-time dry run that baked in gowitness and Chromium), WPScan (and Ruby), testssl.sh (and `dig`, `hexdump`, `ps`), wafw00f, aardwolf and the Python packages only they used (aiodns, aiomultiprocess, aiosqlite, argh, censys, h11, metafinder, netaddr, netlas, pymongo, PyMySQL, scapy, shodan, ujson, uro, uvloop) are gone from `web/Dockerfile` and `web/requirements.txt`, together with the compilers, `libpcap-dev`, `jq`, `netcat`, `wget` and `bzip2` they needed. nuclei stays (the daily template refresh runs it) and is now built in a throwaway stage, so the Go toolchain is no longer part of the image. `celery-entrypoint.sh` no longer runs `naabu -version`, `get_system_status` no longer reports an nmap version, and `scripts/check_tool_versions.py` and `scripts/generate_sbom.py` list nuclei and Go only. Also removed: the unused `auth_checks.certificate_verifies`.
- **The local-fallback machinery is gone.** Every kind is a Sentinel's work and none runs on the server, so `CVENGINE_LOCAL_FALLBACK` is ignored (set or not, a kind waits for a scanner, and nothing warns about the variable; remove it from `.env`), and what only served it is removed: the fallback telemetry (`agentapi.ScannerFallback`, dropped by migration `agentapi.0019`, `manage.py scanner_fallbacks`, the `scanning.scanner.fallbacks` status block), the per-hour local fallback caps (`CVENGINE_LOCAL_FALLBACK_NMAP_PER_HOUR`, `CVENGINE_LOCAL_FALLBACK_NAABU_PER_HOUR`) and warning, the vantage-point and unknown-kind warnings of the scanner status, and the reviewer's `local_share` check. The scanner-offline reading, its strip and the waiting of every stage are unchanged.
- **Dead target-facing code is deleted.** The server's own certificate, TLS and mail-authentication readers have had no caller since the hygiene sweep moved to a Sentinel: `hygiene.probe_tls` (with `_fetch_cert`, `_chain_error` and `_accepts_legacy_tls`), `legacy_tls.py` (the TLS 1.0 client), `hygiene.email_findings` with the DNS readers it used (`fetch_txt`, `fetch_records`, `has_record`, `ask_txt`, `dns_askers`, `consensus_txt`), `mailfail.review` and `mailfail.fetch_mta_sts_policy`. The evaluators, the rating and the storage stay. Their tests moved to the Sentinel repository or were rewritten to run the scanner library's own reader through the sweep.

## v1.3.7 (2026-11-06)

### Fixed

- **A Sentinel can no longer be refused for ever by a call counter that lost its expiry.** The claim, report and heartbeat calls of an agent or Sentinel are counted per minute in a cache key that expires with the minute. A Sentinel that polls a few times a minute was answered `429 too many claim calls: at most 60 per 60 seconds` for an hour while its sibling worked, which only a counter that never resets explains. A counter that is over its limit is now checked once for an expiry, and one that has none is started again. A refusal is also written to the log once per window with the counter's name and count, so a refusal that is a real burst can be told apart from one that is not.
- **A scan no longer waits behind host assessments.** Discovery scans and host assessments share one queue, and the top-up queues assessments by the hundred: on an install with one discovery worker, 22 scans sat queued for 17 hours behind about 630 assessments. Assessments (their first queueing and the reaper's re-queueing) now go out at the lowest priority the broker has, and scans keep the highest, so a scan is the next thing the discovery worker takes. Messages already in the queue keep their place. No migration, no setting to change, nothing to run after the update.
- **A PDF report no longer waits behind the CVE feed or certificate lookups, and a report no worker takes stops spinning.** Reports were queued on the CVE feed worker's queue, whose four slots a certificate lookup in backoff can hold for minutes at a time; on a stack that lost its other workers a report waited for hours with the page saying "working". Reports now have their own queue (`report_queue`), served by the classic worker, and a report still queued after ten minutes is marked failed with a message to try again (it was two hours), and a worker that takes the message late skips it. No migration, no setting to change, nothing to run after the update.

## v1.3.6 (2026-11-05)

### Fixed

- **The all-projects dashboard no longer overloads a small server.** An install with about 4,100 scans on a 3-core VPS ran at a load of 8 and swapped, and nearly all of it was one query repeated: the live ticker's open-ports count, which took 6 to 18 s and, when its snapshot went cold, was run by six requests at once. Three changes:
  - **The three ticker counts (live hosts, addresses, open ports) read the host list once.** They used to be three nested queries that each repeated the whole host walk inside them; the addresses and ports are now looked up through their link tables by id. The numbers are the same (`tests/test_discovery_counts_query.py` checks the new result against the old query).
  - **A computed snapshot is kept for ten minutes, not three times its freshness window, and a request with nothing to serve waits up to ten seconds for the one computing it** (it waited two). A slow count therefore leaves an old copy to serve while one request recomputes it, instead of an empty key that sends every poll to compute it at once. The lock of a computing request lasts 45 s (was 20).
  - **A Live page waits at least twice as long as a slow answer took before it asks again** (`static/cve/poll.js`). It already polled one request at a time, paused in a hidden tab and backed off on errors; it now also backs off when the server is slow but not failing, so a busy server is not met with more of the same question.
- No migration, no setting to change, nothing to run after the update.

## v1.3.5 (2026-11-04)

### Upgrade notes

- **Stored findings are brought in line by themselves after the update: nobody runs a command.** When a worker starts on this version it applies, once per install and in this order, the steps that re-rate, merge and close what was stored under the old rules: `apply_range_corrections` (the sourced CVE range corrections; the CVEs are re-correlated), `reopen_openvas_identity_closures` (OpenVAS matches closed as "different product" by mistake, only automatic closures nobody worked), `dedupe_findings` (scanner and hygiene findings filed more than once), `fold_alias_findings` (what redirect aliases share with their target host), `close_fixed_findings` (WPScan findings past their fix), `rerate_hygiene` (hygiene findings by the endpoint rules; a person's remediation status is never changed), `rerate_dangling_dns` (dangling-DNS findings by what an outsider can claim; read-only DNS and HTTP checks) and `rerate_findings` (last: rescores the hosts, clears the fire page mark of matches that are no longer fire without sending anything, and holds stored classifications to their CVE's `max_remote_state`). One worker runs them, one at a time in the background, and each writes a line with its counts to the activity feed. A step that fails is tried again later, at most three times, and then reported as given up in the activity feed. Each step is safe to run again, never merges across projects, and never touches a finding a person closed or set by hand. Every command is still there to preview what it would change (a dry run without `--apply`). `CVENGINE_AUTO_BACKFILLS=0` turns the automatic run off.
- **Migration `targetApp.0008_domain_expect_private_ca`** adds one column with a database default (`false`), so the previous release keeps working against the new schema.
- **Heat is re-rated as soon as this version runs** (heat is computed when it is read, nothing is migrated); the automatic `rerate_findings` step above rescores the hosts at once. A heat a person set by hand wins over every rule below and is not counted as a change.
- **Re-cut on 2026-11-04.** This release was published again with the automatic steps above and two fixes reported by camstork (#414, #408). An install that already updated to the first 1.3.5 is offered the update again and runs the steps when its workers restart.

### Added

- **Re-rate a finding's heat by hand.** cvENGINE derives a finding's heat (fire, smoke, ice) and had no way to change it when a person who reviewed the finding knew better. A CVE match or a scanner finding can now be set to fire, smoke or ice with a reason, and cleared back to the derived heat: on its detail pane (the **Heat** control under What to do, for anyone who may change its status), over REST (`POST projects/{slug}/findings/{kind}/{id}/heat`, and `POST projects/{slug}/findings/heat` for up to 500 findings with one heat and one reason) and over MCP (`set_finding_heat`, one finding or `items`). The heat set wins everywhere a heat is read: the Findings list, its heat facet and counts, host heat and scores, dashboards, reports, the graph, the validation and OpenVAS order and alerts; `heat_reason` says "set to smoke by alice: <reason>" and findings carry `heat_override` `{heat, reason, by, at}`. Each change is in the finding's audit trail (`event: heat`). A match a change makes fire is paged once like any match that turns fire; a closed one never, and lowering never pages. Triage, AI review and validation never undo it, and a rescan keeps it while the match's evidence is the same. Migration `cveFeed.0142` adds the columns with database defaults, so the previous version keeps working during the update.

### Changed

- **Microsoft 365 discovery and enrolment names are rated as Microsoft's.** msoid, enterpriseenrollment, enterpriseregistration, autodiscover, lyncdiscover and sip names that point at Microsoft (by CNAME, or by a certificate that only names Microsoft) serve Microsoft's own certificate by design: the name mismatch is now info instead of high, and nothing else is reported about such an endpoint (it is not the client's asset; a finding already open on one resolves at the next check).
- **Vendor-managed endpoints are tagged and rated ice or info.** A name that points by CNAME at a vendor platform (Zendesk, Squarespace, Microsoft 365, Palantir, Listrak, GoDaddy Payments, Azure VPN gateway, Railway, Netlify, Vercel and others; the list is data in `cveFeed/data/vendor_platforms.json`) gets the host label `vendor-managed`, and its TLS and SPF findings are rated low at most (certificate trust and name findings info), because the configuration is the vendor's.
- **A target can say its devices use their vendor's private CA.** `POST projects/{slug}/targets/{id}/private-ca` (`expect_private_ca`) and the MCP tool `set_target_private_ca`; when set, an untrusted or self-signed chain on that target is rated info instead of medium. Every target record carries `expect_private_ca`.
- **BREACH is smoke only where there is a secret to steal.** It stays medium when the host sets a session or auth cookie or serves a sign-in or session-protected page (from the route probes); otherwise (static sites, platform error pages) it is low.
- **Certificates on auto-renewing managed platforms with a week or more left are low**, and an expired certificate on a development or test host (dev, test, qa, uat, staging and similar names) is medium instead of critical.
- **A DMARC report-authorisation gap under p=quarantine or p=reject is info**: the policy still stops spoofed mail, only the reports are lost. It stays medium under p=none.
- **Missing SPF on a subdomain under an enforcing DMARC policy is info.** When the name's own policy, or its organizational domain's (`sp=` first), is quarantine or reject, spoofed mail from it already fails DMARC.
- **A parent's weak DMARC policy is one finding on the parent, not one per subdomain.** Subdomains with no record of their own that inherit p=none (or a weak `sp=`) now report it once, on the organizational domain, listing the names it covers (`evidence.covers`).
- **Remediation work lists one item per upgrade.** CVEs on one host that the same upgrade of the same component fixes (CVE-2020-11022 and CVE-2020-11023 on jQuery 3.4.1, both fixed in 3.5.0) are one item of `get_remediation_work` / `GET /work`, carrying `cves`, `match_ids` and `upgrade_to`; `count` counts items and the new `matches` the matches behind them. Each match keeps its own row, status and triage.
- **A version-only CVE match is smoke at most, even when its version is proven.** A version read from the component itself (a jQuery file's own banner, a vendor banner nmap probed, a version-class template) proves the version is there, not that the flaw is exploitable. Fire now needs proof of the flaw: a template hit, a validation hit, the host's own inventory, the distribution's tracker or a runZero report. The heat reason says "version proven ..., but no check has shown the flaw".
- **Exploit-DB alone never lifts a version-only match above smoke**, under the KEV policy and under the legacy policy (even with `CVENGINE_VERSION_ONLY_MAX_HEAT=fire`).
- **Heat follows the finding's triage.** A CVE match or scanner finding whose triage is still pending, or classified needs validation, is smoke at most; one whose validation came back clean is ice at most. A validation hit, or a template hit that showed the flaw, lifts both. When triage stops holding a match that has proof, it is paged once as "confirmed, now fire-tier".
- **A scanner finding rated by its template's severity alone is smoke until a validation reproduces it** (nuclei, OpenVAS and other template scanners; a critical or high finding without a CVE, or any finding under the legacy policy). cvENGINE's own checks (a database that answered without credentials, the node2shell test), bbot findings and manual findings keep their severity heat. The host score counts such a finding at its held heat.
- **Nuclei findings keep the matched request and response,** clipped to 8 KB each (local runs) and 4 KB each (a Sentinel's result), as evidence. A Sentinel may now send the optional `type`, `request` and `response` fields with each nuclei finding; a result without them is accepted and stored as before.
- **The TLS 1.0 / 1.1 finding stores the legacy handshake as its evidence:** the old protocol version the server negotiated and the cipher, with the modern protocol alongside, instead of only the protocol the certificate was read over (which read "TLSv1.3").

### Fixed

- **A delegation nobody hosts at a self-service DNS provider is now fire.** A name delegated to Google Cloud DNS, Route 53, Azure DNS, DigitalOcean, Linode or Hurricane Electric where every delegated name server answers REFUSED or SERVFAIL to a non-recursive SOA query can be claimed by anyone who creates that zone there; baddns rated it medium, so it showed as smoke. It is now high (fire), with the zone and the name servers in the finding. A lame delegation elsewhere stays smoke, and one that a delegated server does serve is ice.
- **Dangling-NS findings are judged at the client's own zone cut.** baddns followed a client's CNAME into the provider's zone (Railway, Marigold) and reported how the provider's name servers answer as a dangling delegation of the client. cvENGINE now walks the delegation down from the registry; a name of the client's that is a CNAME is not a delegation the client made, and that finding is no longer recorded.
- **A dangling CNAME no outsider can claim is ice.** A CNAME whose target does not resolve was smoke even when nobody else can create the target (a load balancer's random name, a tenant name the provider assigns, a domain that is still registered, the client's own domain). It is now low (ice) with the reason, unless the target is at a provider documented as claimable (`cveFeed/data/takeover_providers.json`, from can-i-take-over-xyz) or its registrable domain is not registered.
- **Mailgun "takeover" findings that every host name gets are dropped.** Mailgun answers "404 page not found" for any host, so baddns's HTTP match reported live, claimed Mailgun tracking names as takeovers. An HTTP-match finding now sends a control request with a random host name to the same addresses and is not recorded when the control gets the same answer (a target at a provider listed as claimable is not controlled: its unclaimed page is the same for every unknown host by design).
- **Dangling-DNS findings name their host.** A finding on a name that does not resolve was stored with no host although it says where it matched; it now gets that host.
- **No bucket names are guessed from a domain that belongs to someone else.** The cloud storage sweep guessed bucket names from every target's label, so a target domain owned by another company (say, a retailer's brand that shares the client's word) produced public buckets of that company reported as the client's. Before guessing, cvENGINE now compares the domain with the project's other domains: a different published registrant (RDAP), or name servers and mail servers on another organization's own infrastructure that none of the project's other domains use, means no guesses for it; the sweep's activity line says which domain was skipped and why. Earlier guess findings for such a domain resolve at the next sweep.
- **Identical findings get the same AI classification and severity step.** The stored answer of a scanner, route or hygiene finding was keyed on its evidence but not on everything its prompt showed (a page title, a path, the check's detail), so an answer that closed a finding or lowered its severity was kept to the host it was reached on: the identical finding on another host of the same project asked the model again (a hosted model's temperature is not pinned) or showed no step, and twins ended up at -1, 0 and +1. The key now carries exactly what the prompt shows, so one project's identical findings take one answer on every host, and when another organization's answer may not be used, the project asks one question for all its hosts instead of one per host. The rules between organizations are unchanged (an answer still never closes, lowers or, outside CVE matches, raises another organization's finding). After the update each such finding is asked again once under its new key, within the daily AI caps, and keeps its current classification until the answer lands.
- **A day passing is no longer a new AI question.** The keys AI answers are stored under now ignore what changes on its own between scans (dates and times, a certificate's expiry date, counters, clock-bearing headers such as Date or Set-Cookie, nonces and session ids in excerpts) and keep a certificate's days left only as its bucket (expired, under 7 days, under 30 days, 30 days or more). An expiring certificate used to be asked about again every day; it is now asked again only when it crosses a bucket. The prompt still shows the exact values. Expiring and expired certificate findings get one fresh answer under their new key after the update.
- **The AI reads an SPF record with its qualifiers.** The cleaning applied to host text dropped "?" and "~", so a record ending in `?all` reached the model as a bare `all` and was described as `+all`. The record is now parsed term by term and shown exactly as published, with its closing `all` term and what a receiver gets (neutral, soft fail, fail, pass).
- **AI triage text no longer contradicts what cvENGINE parsed.** The prompt now carries parsed facts: the SPF record and its closing qualifier, the registrable domain of each name in a hygiene finding's evidence and whether it is the finding host's own (the host is redacted, so the model could not compare and called two different domains the same), and, for a medium or higher template finding, the products the host's fingerprint names (the model named a product from the template's tags). A sentence of the model's reason, impact or severity reason that contradicts one of these facts is removed, the reason says what was corrected, and a severity step given with it is dropped. The classification rules are unchanged.
- **A one-off DNS failure no longer opens a "missing record" finding.** "No SPF record" and "No DMARC record" (also the organizational domain's DMARC lookup) are no longer opened on one lookup, and a timeout or SERVFAIL is never read as "no record". By default the first sighting is stored as `pending`, with the DNS answer as its evidence (`evidence.dns`), and the finding opens only when the next hygiene run sees the record missing too; if the next run finds it, the pending row is resolved. `GET .../hygiene?status=pending` lists the held-back ones. No DNS query goes anywhere but the system resolver. To confirm in one run instead, set `CVENGINE_HYGIENE_CONFIRM_RESOLVERS` to extra resolvers (for example `1.1.1.1,8.8.8.8`): a missing record then opens as soon as two resolvers agree. Opting in sends each name whose record seems missing to those resolvers.
- **SPF behind a CNAME is no longer reported missing after one failed lookup.** The SPF record of a name that is an alias (a help-desk or mail vendor's host) is the alias target's, read through the CNAME by each resolver; the alias is now kept in the evidence, and one resolver's failure no longer opens "No SPF record" (see above).
- **A JavaScript library's version comes only from its own file.** bbot's Shodan InternetDB data and the nuclei technology pass named jQuery and Bootstrap at versions they had read from other scripts: another plugin's `?ver=` string or banner (a menu plugin's "jQuery Plugin - v1.2.1"), jQuery Migrate's `?ver=3.4.1`, a forms plugin's `?ver=3.2.1`, a theme's `?ver=1.0.4`, and a different one on each scan, so the stored jQuery version flipped between scans. Those tools now store the library's name without a version; the version is the one the route check and the html-libraries probe read from the library's own file (its banner, or `jquery:"x.y.z"` in its code). Versions an earlier scan stored stay capped candidates until the host's next scan replaces them.
- **WordPress is never read at its jQuery file's version.** A core version (WordPress, Drupal, a forum) equal to a version a JavaScript library's own file on the same host states is that library's number and is not matched, even when it is the core's only version, unless a probe read the core at that version. A host recorded as "WordPress 3.7.1" because core's jQuery is 3.7.1 no longer matches WordPress 3.7.1's advisories.
- **Script tags are read the way a browser reads the page.** The script names used as hints (`fingerprint.script_sources`) came from a pattern that also took a `<script src>` inside an HTML comment, inside another script's text, or after a `<script>` that never closes; they now come from the same tokenizer as the script read. A script now ends only at `</script` followed by a space, `/` or `>`, as in a browser (`</script-x>` is text).
- **The nuclei technology pass stores the same version on every run.** When a template extracted several versions, the first one nuclei returned was kept, in no fixed order; the highest is now kept.
- **X-AspNet-Version is the .NET Framework.** The header (sent only by classic ASP.NET; `2.0.50727`, `4.0.30319` are CLR build lines) was stored as "ASP.NET" with that version, which correlation read as "ASP.NET Core 2.1" and matched to CVE-2023-38180. It is stored as ".NET Framework CLR" now, and such a stored reading of an earlier build is read the same way, so it matches neither ASP.NET Core nor a .NET Framework release range it cannot place.
- **CVE-2020-11022 covers jQuery from 1.2.** Its CNA record and GHSA-gxr4-xjj5-5px2 (both edited in July 2026) start the range at 1.12.0, which missed pages on jQuery 1.2 to 1.11.x; NVD's configuration (from 1.2) and Exploit-DB 49766 (a working exploit for jQuery 1.2) show the wider range. cvENGINE now applies sourced range corrections (`cveFeed.range_corrections`) wherever a CVE's ranges are stored; each corrected block names the bound it replaced and its sources, and a correction stops applying as soon as a feed changes that bound again.
- **An advisory's ranges are listed in full.** The agent API listed at most 20 ranges per affected product, so CVE-2026-119977 (25 WordPress branches, through 7.1.3) looked as if its record stopped at 6.6.10 and its 7.1.3 match came from the description. Up to 60 are listed, with `ranges_total` when there are more. Matching always used the full record; the description never extends it.
- **CVE-2023-5561's template no longer reports patched WordPress.** The template only checks that the REST user list answers, which patched sites do too. Its hit is recorded (as a scanner finding, or as a template confirmation of a CVE match) only when the host's WordPress version is 6.3.1 or older, or unknown; a version a probe read wins over an older fingerprint (`cveFeed.template_gates`).
- **A KEV match is raised only on a version read in the last day.** A new KEV match made from a technology fingerprint more than 24 hours old, which no probe or route check has read again since at the same version, is held instead of created, and the host's version is read again (its front page, generator and feed: the web probes a scan runs), then the host is correlated again. The match is raised on that fresh reading, or by the next scan; a host that moved past the range never gets it. Matches that already exist are updated as before.
- **A rescan updates a scanner finding instead of filing a copy.** Every scan filed a finding it saw again as a new row and left the earlier scans' rows open beside it, so one host could show the same WordPress advisories or the SSH Terrapin finding eight times, and a scan loaded twice doubled the list. A finding seen again (the same project, host name, source, template, name, URL and matcher) is now the same row, moved onto the scan that saw it: its first-seen date, status, note, assignee, comments, audit trail and AI triage stay with it. A status a person set still sticks. Existing copies are merged by `dedupe_findings`: the copy in the host's current scan stays, takes the earliest first-seen date, every copy's comments and audit trail and (when it has none of its own) a person's status and assignee, and each other live copy is closed with a note naming it; copies people worked differently are left open and counted, closed copies are never touched.
- **A hygiene finding is filed once per host, not once per target.** A host covered by its apex's target and by a target of its own had each certificate or TLS finding twice. A second target's check now updates the row the first one filed (same project, host and check). `dedupe_findings` merges the existing pairs, keeping the oldest.
- **A later WPScan that reads a version past an advisory's fix closes that finding as fixed, automatically.** The rows of WordPress core, plugin and theme advisories stayed open after a later scan of the same host read the fixed release (7.1.4). Now an open row whose advisory states a fixed version at or below the version just read is closed as fixed with `closed_automatically` true and a note giving the version, the host and the scan; an "outdated" row closes once the component is no longer outdated; an older-version copy of an advisory still open at the new version is folded into the new row. A row a person set (any status, or reopened by hand) is never touched, and a component whose version was not read proves nothing. An automatic closure is undone if a later scan reports the finding again; a person's fixed status is not. `close_fixed_findings` applies this to what is stored. (CVE matches already closed when the version left the affected range.)
- **A host that only redirects to another host of the project is folded into it.** www, blog or careers names that answer every front page with a permanent redirect (301 or 308) to the apex had findings of their own that were the apex's. A finding such an alias shares with its target (the same scanner template, or the same hygiene check on the same port and, for a certificate, the same certificate) is now closed with a note naming the target's row, after each scan and hygiene check; a finding only the alias has stays open, a temporary redirect or a redirect out of the project is not an alias, and a host that serves its own page again gets its findings back. `fold_alias_findings` applies this to what is stored.
- **A name that is a CNAME no longer gets mail findings of its own.** A CNAME cannot sit beside an MX record, so the MX a lookup returned for www (a CNAME to the apex) was the apex's, and the email checks (Direct Send and the rest) filed the apex's findings again under www. Such names are left out of the email review; the next hygiene check resolves their existing mail findings.
- **Triage no longer classifies a CVE match above what its detection strategy allows from outside.** The library rule confirmed a page's jQuery although the CVE's strategy says a check from outside decides it likely at most (`validation.max_remote_state`); the rules now hold every remote classification to that limit (a validation hit, the host's own inventory and the distribution's tracker are not held, and a template hit is kept at likely at least). "Overridden" now means the shown classification differs from what the model said.
- **An echo port no longer reads as a database or message broker.** A port that sends back whatever it receives matched network templates such as `unauth-psql` and `apache-rocketmq-broker-unauth`, because their matcher words are in their own request. A protocol-template match is now set aside when its response equals or contains the request it sent, when nmap names the port `echo`, or when one port matched two or more unrelated protocol templates in one run. The run's activity line counts what was set aside and why.
- **A match on the page a host returns for everything is set aside.** An open-proxy template fired on a load balancer with no backend, where every request gets the same "503 No server is available" page. An HTTP match above info is now set aside when the responses nuclei recorded for it are all the same body, or when its error response (4xx/5xx) is exactly what the host returns for a path no site serves (one control request, through the same safe-address gate as the other probes; a control that fails keeps the finding).
- **FTP, Telnet and the r-services need a greeting to be reported.** A front end that accepts a connection on every port and closes it was reported as FTP and rlogin because nmap names ports 21 and 513 from its port table. A cleartext-service finding now needs a banner or nmap's fingerprint of the service, and on an address with 25 or more silent open ports (one that accepts everything) it needs a banner. Findings without one resolve at the next hygiene run.
- **"No forward secrecy" on a listener that completes no handshake becomes "TLS listener does not negotiate".** When testssl.sh finds no protocol offered, its cipher results describe no handshake, not a weak setup: the port gets one low finding saying it does not negotiate instead, and its earlier cipher findings resolve.
- **"No TLS_FALLBACK_SCSV" is no longer reported when the downgraded handshake never completes.** testssl.sh flags a server that refuses the downgrade with a handshake failure instead of the standard alert (and says only "likely"); no downgraded session can be established, so it is not a finding.
- **OpenVAS CVE matches no longer close themselves as "different product".** An OpenVAS match is stored under its NVT and port ("OpenVAS NVT <oid> on 22/tcp"), and the product-identity check compared that name with the advisory's products, so every match an NVT did not confirm with a hard quality of detection (most of an unauthenticated scan: remote banner results at QoD 30) was closed as not affected minutes after the import, with a reason that was not true. The NVT tested that product itself, so OpenVAS matches are no longer compared by name (reported by camstork, #414).
- **An OpenVAS match records what the NVT identified.** The version the NVT printed ("Installed version:") is now the match's version, and the detected product's CPE, the product, the installed and the fixed version are kept in its evidence beside the OID and port (reported by camstork, #414).
- **Triage sees the scanner's evidence for an OpenVAS match.** The AI review is given the NVT's name, the port, the quality of detection and its type, the product and versions it identified and the start of its result text (at most 600 characters, cleaned like any other host text: the host's names redacted, secrets masked, sentences of instructions dropped), instead of only the opaque "OpenVAS NVT <oid> on <port>" (reported by camstork, #414).
- **A confirmed OpenVAS match is attributed to OpenVAS.** Its triage reason said "a nuclei template check matched this host"; it now says which check confirmed it, for example "an OpenVAS check (QoD 98, remote_vul) confirmed it on this host" (reported by camstork, #414).
- **A service nmap could not name a product for is now probed again at full intensity.** An open TCP port with a service name but no product (for example a DNS server on 5353 that nmap's normal intensity only soft-matched as "generic dns response: REFUSED") was never followed up, so a product such as ISC BIND and its version were never found and none of its CVEs could match. Such a port is now a lead for the existing `nmap -sV --version-all` probe, behind the probes for products that have no version yet: it never takes their place under the per-host limit of five, and is probed at a later scan once theirs are done. Web ports (left to the web probes) and `tcpwrapped` ports are not included, and the probe keeps the same scope, exclusion and address checks (reported by camstork, #408).
- **A port nmap had identified by probing no longer takes the port table's name from a later sweep.** When nmap's probing of a port ended with a soft match but did not finish, the service name it found (for example `domain` on 5353) was replaced by the port-number guess (`mdns`) at the next run that only saw the port open. The stored name and details are now kept (reported by camstork, #408).

## v1.3.4 (2026-11-03)

### Upgrade notes

- **No migration and no manual step.** The host watcher, which cron starts every minute, runs the new `scripts/update-watch.sh` within a minute of the update, and from then on carries out cvENGINE's gvmd restart requests (see Fixed). Sentinels get their half (hevnsnt/cvENGINE-sentinel#32) by their own self-update; a Sentinel that has not updated yet still reports a stale-cache run as before, and this server then reads it as before.
- **An installation without a Redis password gets one during this update.** The updater writes `REDIS_PASSWORD` to `.env` (and the Compose env file the installation uses) and recreates Redis with it, then web and the workers as every update does; tasks queued at that moment are lost once. An installation that already has a password is unchanged.

### Security

A security review of everything changed since 1.3.0 (access control and tenancy, the Sentinel channel and the host scripts, outbound requests and the screenshot browser, commands and templates, AI and secrets, the browser side). Fixed:

- **Every installation's broker gets a password by itself.** An installation set up before `REDIS_PASSWORD` existed kept an open Redis broker until someone ran `make redis-auth`, and `docker-compose.hostnet.yml` publishes that broker on the host's loopback for the celery container, where any local user or process on the Docker host could queue work the workers run as root. The update (in-app, `scripts/update.sh`, automatic deployment) and `make up` now generate `REDIS_PASSWORD` when it is empty (`scripts/redis-password.sh`: written to `.env` and to the installation's Compose env file) and recreate Redis with it together with the services that use it. Nothing to do by hand; headless installations are covered.
- **The updater no longer reads `web/.commit` through a link.** `scripts/apply-update.sh` read the running build's commit with `head`, which follows a symlink a compromised web container could plant there, and copied the first line of the file it pointed at (any root-readable host file) into the update log the app shows. It is now read through `scripts/update-state.py` (no links, a regular file, a commit id or nothing).
- **One organization's page can no longer raise another organization's findings.** A stored AI answer that raises attention (likely, a severity step up) was shared with every organization's finding of the same signature; for every kind but a CVE match the prompt showed host text the signature does not carry (a page title, a bbot description, a nuclei path), so a host one organization controls could steer another organization's routes and scanner findings up a step. Such an answer is now shared only within its project; elsewhere the finding asks its own question. A CVE match is unchanged (its shown host text is part of its signature). Answers already applied are shown without the borrowed severity step at once and are replaced at the finding's next review.
- **Copied curl commands make one request.** curl expands `[1-100000]` and `{a,b}` in a URL even inside quotes, so a page whose script address carried such a pattern turned the "read the file" command in Confirm it yourself into thousands of requests from the tester's machine. Every curl cvENGINE shows a person now passes `-g` (no URL globbing).
- **The daily operating-system path probe respects scope.** It chose up to eight recently fingerprinted addresses from every organization and sent each two raw packets, including addresses excluded or archived since, held only by manual-only or passive targets, or outside a bug bounty program's scope. It now picks only an address some project may still have probed (a scanning target's host, not excluded or archived, and within the project's own scope).
- **An overlong CVE id from a Sentinel is dropped**, instead of failing the whole nuclei stage or OpenVAS import of its project (CVE ids are bounded to seven digits, and the finding store skips one longer than its column).

### Fixed

- **An update no longer stops because a busy worker answered the pause late.** The updater gave each worker one five-second window to confirm it had stopped taking new work, and stopped the update (rolling it back) when no answer came. A worker that has just started is busy with its correlation and template passes and can miss that window. It is now asked up to three times (5, 15 and 30 seconds).

- **The gvmd restart request runs once on an add-on started by hand.** `scripts/openvas-sync.sh restart-gvmd` gave up before answering when `web/.openvas-wanted` did not exist (`make openvas-up`), so the watcher started it again every two seconds and gvmd was never restarted. It now restarts gvmd when it is running and the add-on is not switched off, and always answers.

- **OpenVAS: results whose CVEs gvmd left out no longer close earlier findings** (reported by camstork, #394). gvmd 26.35 and later reload the NVT cache their GMP answers are built from only in their periodic-jobs process (greenbone/gvmd#3220), so after the first feed load, and for NVTs a later feed sync adds, every result came without its CVE references. cvENGINE read that as "no CVE", counted the host as scanned clean and closed what earlier OpenVAS scans had found on it. A result with CERT-Bund or DFN-CERT references (which gvmd links to an NVT through its CVEs) and no CVE reference now marks the run as read from a stale cache: it is imported as partial (nothing earlier is resolved), the GVM task and report are kept, the hosts are asked again in three days, and the run says to restart gvmd. With the host's watcher installed, cvENGINE asks it to restart `openvas-gvmd` (at most once in six hours; `scripts/openvas-sync.sh restart-gvmd`, answered in `web/.openvas-restarted`). A Sentinel reports the same count (`nvt_cache_stale`), and its run is treated the same way.

## v1.3.3 (2026-11-03)

### Thanks

- **[camstork](https://github.com/camstork)**, our most thorough beta tester. Since 1.3.0 they have filed sixteen reports, each with a reproduction, the root cause traced to the line, and a suggested fix: cve.now branch ranges (#205), rescans revalidating every match (#206), the seed-import feed gap (#207), the Nuclei tag prompt (#208), local and OpenAI-compatible AI providers (#209), the triage ceiling for version-only evidence (#210), the update banner on forks (#211), private addresses scored as public (#212), catch-all web servers (#213), Docker Desktop OS guesses (#214), gzip pages from embedded devices (#281), cleartext administration services (#282), aliases and notes (#283), discovery modes (#284), KEV revalidation after rescans (#338) and the OpenVAS import on gvmd 22.7 (#394). Much of what 1.3.1, 1.3.2 and 1.3.3 fix, they found first. Thank you.

### Upgrade notes

- **kombu, amqp, billiard and vine are now pinned** (`web/requirements.txt`: 5.7.0, 5.4.2, 4.3.2, 5.1.0, what CI and the images already installed). kombu decides how a dropped Redis connection is handled; a bump should run `tests/test_gevent_worker_e2e.py` first. If kombu ever lacks what `reNgine/broker_acks.py` patches, the worker logs a warning with its version and starts with kombu's own acknowledgements.
- **The celery container gets a seccomp profile, so the screenshot browser can use Chromium's sandbox; screenshots stay off until it does.** `docker-compose.yml` (and `docker-compose.dev.yml`) now give the `celery` service Playwright's own seccomp profile, `config/seccomp/playwright-chromium.json` (Docker's default plus the `clone`, `setns` and `unshare` calls user namespaces need, vendored unchanged from Playwright v1.64.1; source and checksum in THIRD-PARTY.md). `make up`, `scripts/update.sh` and the in-app updater recreate the container, so the update applies it with no manual step; a compose file of your own, or a container started by hand, needs the same `security_opt` line. The celery container checks the sandbox at every start (one line in `docker compose logs celery`: "Screenshot sandbox: engaged" or "FAILED" with the reason). **If it cannot start** (the profile is missing, or the host kernel does not allow unprivileged user namespaces), screenshots are off: no page is opened, and the host page, the Take screenshot button, `get_system_status` and the activity feed say why. Fix the cause and recreate the container (`docker compose up -d celery`), or, accepting the risk, set `CVENGINE_SCREENSHOT_SANDBOX=0` in `.env` to run the browser without its sandbox as before (**unsafe**: a renderer exploit then runs as `nobody` in the celery container, with its network; the status shows a problem while it is set). To stop screenshots altogether instead (also the stopgap for an install that cannot update yet), set `CVENGINE_SCREENSHOTS=0`. The scan results volume becomes `root:101`, mode 0750, at every start of the celery container (root and the proxy's nginx only): a proxy of your own that reads the volume directly under another account would need the same group.
- **Rules-written CVE detection records are rewritten once, automatically.** The periodic detection task (and `manage.py author_cve_detections`) regenerates every record the rules wrote under an older schema, a few thousand a run, with the class-aware rules (see Changed). AI-written and person-written records are never touched. A record imported later from an older seed keeps its schema version and is picked up the same way. No migration.
- **Migration `cveFeed` 0140 reads every stored triage answer once.** It records where each was reached (task 256) and takes the model's words off the shared correlation table and the matches it seeded. Allow about 38 seconds per 100,000 stored answers; it adds an index to the provider-call ledger first. The first correlation after it then reviews, once, what earlier versions shared across organizations (see Fixed).
- **Technology-scan library matches on a host that only redirects close by themselves, without a rescan of the matches; earlier file-based closures reopen once.** The reconcile sweep (every five minutes) now also re-checks the open technology-scan matches of every project against their host's own front page, 500 a run in id order from a cursor of its own and at most two minutes a run (about 144,000 matches a day; each host's page is read once a run for all its matches), and closes, with a stored reason that is never shared with another host, one kind only (see Fixed): a host whose every front page answers a permanent redirect (301 or 308) to another host the project inventories (`redirect_alias`), at the first sweep when its route check is less than 8 days old. The page's own files never close a match: they inform its note and keep it at confidence 0.4. At the first correlation after the upgrade, library matches an earlier build closed on the page's files, an absence or a cache-buster, and validation's fixed-version script-read closures, are reopened once (an automatic closure on a match nobody worked; never a redirect alias or a person's closure), with no page sent; the log line and one activity line per project give the counts. Matches a person or a check vouches for (worked, assigned, commented, AI-confirmed, validated), on this scan's row of the host or an earlier one, are never closed, and a closed match reopens on a fresh reading that no longer supports it.
- **A front page's route state may change once after the update.** The route check now reads a front page up to 300 KB (was 64 KB), so its login, single sign-on and meta-refresh markers and the sensitive-data scan see the whole page. A front page whose login form or a sensitive value sits past the first 64 KB is recorded once as changed (for example open to login) at its next check; nothing else changes, and other routes are still read up to 64 KB.
- **No default change in what is scanned.** Port 25 is still scanned by the server's own scanners. New by default: never more than 400 charged connection attempts to port 25 from this server in any 60 minutes (the rest are probed later, not dropped); on naabu's path an address is no longer probed on port 25 because a chunk neighbour has it open and at most 30 nmap and 10 naabu local fallback stages in each UTC clock hour (the rest run when their target is next scanned). `CVENGINE_LOCAL_PORT_BUDGETS=` (empty) and the two fallback caps at `0` give the behaviour of before. No migration: the counters, the log and the owed ports live in the broker's Redis (a restart without persistence loses them; an owed port then waits for its address's next regular rescan). Not counted or paced: nuclei network, ssl and javascript templates on the ports they declare, bbot's deep preset, OpenVAS, UDP, and this server's own mail notifications.
- **Four additive migrations, no manual step:** `cveFeed` 0138 (the False positive status on supplier findings; matches and findings already store it as text), 0139 (records a heat-policy baseline so nothing already fire pages again under the new rule) 0140 (where each stored triage answer was reached; see the entry above for its run time) and 0141 (a database default for the two new NOT NULL columns, so the old code still running during the update can keep inserting). One-off steps run by themselves after the update: findings an answer shared across organizations closed are reviewed once, the rules-written detection records are rewritten, earlier file-based library closures reopen without paging, and proven library matches are settled as confirmed in the background.

### Added

- **Confirm it yourself, for this match on this host.** The **Confirm it yourself** fold of a CVE match now starts with guidance built from the match's own evidence, before the general guidance for the CVE (which moves into a fold of its own): where to look, with links (the script URL the page loaded and every other copy of the library on it, the page that loads it, the host and port, the header or path a probe read, the plugin's `readme.txt`), what to look for (the file's first comment line, `Server: nginx/1.18.0`, `Stable tag: 2.3.0`, the testssl.sh test), read-only steps with the real host, port and URL filled in (never `<TARGET>` or `<PORT>`: `curl` of the script, a browser-console expression such as `jQuery.fn.jquery` or `jQuery.ui.version`, `nmap -sV -p 22 <address>`, `nc` for an SSH greeting, `testssl.sh --renegotiation host:port`; the nuclei template that matched is offered apart, as an active check, `nuclei -ni -id <template>`), how to read the answer (affected, not affected, can't tell, against every affected release line: a version is cleared only outside all of them, and the fix names the fixed release of its own line and the others), what makes it exploitable beyond the version (the advisory's own sentence, quoted, and a curated reading for the most common jQuery and jQuery UI CVEs, with a `grep` for the calls the site's own code makes to the affected functions), the application's dependency checks (npm, Composer, Maven/WebJars, RubyGems, NuGet, PyPI) and a search for bundled copies, the host package and the distribution trackers where a backport can apply, the nuclei template that ran (a link to it, the request it sent and what matched) and the fix. Two harmless browser-console self-tests are curated for CVE-2020-11022 and CVE-2019-11358: they run only in the tester's own tab, change nothing on the server and execute no script. A copy the page shadows, or loads only as a fallback, gets neither the console step nor a self-test (both speak for the copy that runs last): the guide points to that copy's own file. The nuclei hit shown is only ever the match's own project's, with credentials in its request masked. Every command is built by cvENGINE from validated values and shell-quoted; banners, headers, page text and testssl.sh or nuclei output are shown as quoted data and never reach a command. The same guide is in `get_finding` (REST and MCP) as `confirm`, and in the PDF report as **How to confirm it** for a finding that is not confirmed.
- **Close a finding as a false positive, and say why when closing anything.** CVE matches, scanner findings and hygiene findings have a new remediation status, **False positive** (`false_positive`): the finding is not real on this host, as opposed to fixed or an accepted risk. It is closed exactly like those two: out of the actionable Findings list, the host's heat, score and exposure counts, fire notifications, the OpenVAS host selection and the remediation work list, and a rescan keeps it closed (the next scan's copy inherits the status and the note, and a hygiene finding found again stays closed; only a fixed one reopens as a regression). Every closure (fixed, accepted risk, false positive) now asks for a note on why in a note box, on a single finding, from a row's menu and for a bulk change (the note goes on every finding changed); the UI will not mark a false positive without one, while the API takes an empty note. The note (at most 2,000 characters, stripped, control characters removed) is stored with who and when, written to the finding's audit trail and the activity feed, shown under the status in the detail pane and as the status chip's tooltip, exported in the Findings CSV, and printed in PDF reports with closed findings ("Why it was closed", with a count by status). REST and MCP (`update_remediation_status`, `update_finding_status`, `bulk_update_finding_status`, the finding lists and detail) carry `closure_note`, `closed_by`, `closed_at` and `closed_automatically`; a CVE match the engine closed on its own (ruled out by the AI or a correlation rule, a scanner finding no longer reported, a hygiene finding no longer found) shows the engine's own reason there and is never labelled a false positive. The Findings status filter adds **Closed** and **False positive** (`findings?status=closed|false_positive|...`), and the Matches filter every status and any closed. Supplier findings can be closed as a false positive too (`set_supplier_finding_status`, `PATCH .../suppliers/findings/{id}`, organization admin as before): the weekly check then leaves it closed. Migration `cveFeed` 0138 widens the supplier finding's status and note and adds when its status was set; nothing is backfilled and existing statuses and notes are unchanged ([Closing a finding](docs/AGENT-API.md#triage-and-remediation)).
- **A ledger of this server's own outbound connections.** cvENGINE now counts the new outbound TCP connections its own scanners open (nmap, naabu, testssl.sh, nuclei, the in-process probes), per hour for seven days: per watched port (25, 465, 587, 23 and 3389 by default, `CVENGINE_LOCAL_WATCH_PORTS`), for all ports, and per scan kind and project. `get_system_status` (MCP and `GET /api/v1/system/status`) shows the last hour, 24 hours and seven days under `egress`; the external tools are counted from their probe plan, which the output says is an estimate. A provider that asks how many connections to port 25 your server opens can now be answered ([Cloud hosts and port 25](docs/CONFIGURATION.md#cloud-hosts-and-port-25)).
- **Every local connection to the mail ports is logged.** One record per outbound connection this server's own scanners open to port 25, 465 or 587 (`CVENGINE_EGRESS_LOG_PORTS`): the time, the destination address and port, sent, paced or skipped, the tool, project and scan, and whether the record is exact (this server opened or refused that very connection) or estimated (nmap, naabu, testssl.sh or nuclei was given it in its probe plan; their own packets are not seen one by one). Kept 14 days and at most 200,000 records in Redis (`CVENGINE_EGRESS_LOG_DAYS`, `CVENGINE_EGRESS_LOG_MAX`), oldest dropped first. App Admins read it with `GET /api/v1/egress/connections` or `list_egress_connections` (filters port, project, since, until; paged) and download it as CSV from Settings, configMaxxing, **Outbound connections**.
- **Port 25 is paced, never skipped.** A shared budget (`CVENGINE_LOCAL_PORT_BUDGETS`, default `25:400/hour`; the port-25 number also on Settings, configMaxxing, **Outbound connections**) is asked before a local scan touches port 25: never more than 400 charged connection attempts in any 60 minutes, and never more than 100 in any 15 minutes, or one testssl.sh run and nothing else (one window across workers, on the Redis server's clock; attempts are charged when each process starts, after its memory wait: nmap per chunk, naabu per group, testssl.sh per run, nuclei per run, the paced pass per run; a process with no room then runs without port 25 and the port is owed or stays due). A testssl.sh run is charged 240 (measured: 159 to 231 connections with testssl.sh 3.2.4 and this app's arguments, rounded up), whole and alone: only into 15 minutes with no other port-25 grant, and nothing else is granted in the 15 after it (a budget below 240 means no STARTTLS test on port 25 from this server); a nuclei `host:25` target is charged 25 (an estimate), whole; nmap and naabu one per address whose plan includes port 25 (the SYN; nmap adds a few connections to an open port 25, and retries are not counted). Within the budget the scan is the scan of before, except that on naabu's path an address is probed on port 25 only when port 25 is open on it, no longer because a chunk neighbour has it open. Over it, the remaining addresses are scanned now without port 25 and a paced pass probes port 25 on them as the budget allows, so every host still gets it from this server, spread over time (`port 25 paced: n of m hosts this hour` in the activity log, and a line at 50, 80 and 100 percent of the hour's budget); a nuclei `host:25` target not granted comes back with the host's next nuclei run, and `host:25` targets take turns (least recently admitted first). A testssl.sh run on port 25 that has waited 60 minutes for its quiet 15 minutes gets priority: ordinary port-25 grants are held until it starts (within at most another 61 minutes). Sentinels are never paced.
- **A cap on stages that fall back to the server, and a warning when they do.** At most 30 nmap and 10 naabu stages in each UTC clock hour run here because no Sentinel took them, including when every Sentinel the project has for the work is offline (`CVENGINE_LOCAL_FALLBACK_NMAP_PER_HOUR`, `_NAABU_`, configMaxxing; `0` is unlimited; a project that never had a Sentinel scans here as before, uncapped); over the cap the addresses stay due and run when that target is next scanned. Five or more fallbacks in the last 60 minutes writes `N stages fell back to local execution in the last hour: check your Sentinels` with the Sentinels that are offline or full, and `get_system_status` lists it as a problem while it lasts.
- **An opt-in skip list for this server.** `CVENGINE_LOCAL_SKIP_PORTS` (empty by default) names ports the server never connects to: local nmap and naabu exclude them, testssl.sh STARTTLS, probes and validation checks on them are not run here, and check coverage says `skipped on the server: port 25`. A Sentinel still scans them.
- **Choose, when you add a target, that it is exactly one host.** In **Add targets**, start a line with `=` (`=example.com`, `=app.example.com`) to add just that host: no subdomain is discovered, kept or scanned under it, and its parent domain is never promoted. The dialog also has a **Scope** choice for the whole paste (Auto, This host only, Include the parent domain); a `=` line overrides it. The agent API and MCP take the same mark and an optional `scope_mode` on `add_target`, `add_targets` and `add_targets_by_org` (`POST .../targets`, `.../targets/bulk`, `targets/bulk-org`), validated like `set_target_scope`, and echo the stored scope. Nothing changes for a target added without the mark or the choice: `example.com` still covers everything under it, and existing targets keep their scope. A target set to **this host only** (`scope_mode` `host`, now or before) is enforced as exactly one host at every boundary: bbot's events, the certificate watch (which no longer queries crt.sh for it), reuse of a parent's discovery, the new-hosts pipeline and Rescan hosts, the nuclei passes, the TLS and email hygiene checks, the testssl audit, OpenVAS, the thorough WPScan pass, finding validation (automatic and requested), an agent's network checks, the full port and UDP sweeps, the virtual host and subdirectory application sweeps, the web archive lookup and the scheduled screenshots ([Exact-host targets](docs/PIPELINE.md#exact-host-targets)). No migration.

### Changed

- **The per-CVE detection records check each product the way that fits it.** The rules author sorts every product an advisory names into a class (a client-side JavaScript library, a language package of npm, PyPI, Maven, RubyGems, Packagist, NuGet, Go or crates.io, an operating-system package, a distribution release, a CMS extension, a network product) and writes checks that fit: no nmap banner check and no `dpkg`, `rpm` or `apk` check for a JavaScript library's packagings or a language package (their versions are read from the page's script files and from the application's lockfile or package manager); an OS package is checked with its own distribution's package manager under its binary name (`openssh-server`), against the distribution's own build, and a distribution release (`Red Hat Enterprise Linux 9`) or a container image is never a banner; a web server's version is read from its response header; a CMS extension named like a library (Drupal's Bootstrap theme) is not read as the library. The packagings of one library (jQuery, npm `jquery`, Composer `components/jquery`, Maven `org.webjars.npm:jquery`, RubyGems `jquery-rails`, NuGet `jQuery`) are one component that lists them. The confirm steps and the true and false positive signs are written per class (no backport language for a served JavaScript file), and the advisory's own condition for exploitation is kept, quoted, as `exploit_precondition`. The AI guidance prompt gets the same class rules. The schema version is 4 (new `components`, `exploit_precondition` and the `bundled_copy` host check); older records stay valid, and every record is shown with the current renderer, so an older record's `dpkg` command for an npm packaging is no longer displayed.
- **What this server scans by itself is metered and paced.** Before, a stage no Sentinel took in time ran here at full speed, and nmap and naabu touched port 25 on every address at once; a VPS provider (Contabo) warned about the rate of outbound connections to port 25 and threatened to block the port.
