Nothing that was scanned before is skipped by default: the same ports are scanned, local runs within the budget have the same command lines, and only bursts over the budget are spread out. See the Added entries above and [Cloud hosts and port 25](docs/CONFIGURATION.md#cloud-hosts-and-port-25).
- **Heat follows one rule: on the KEV and proven is fire, on the KEV and inferred is smoke, anything else is ice.** A CVE on the KEV (CISA KEV, ENISA EUVD or VulnCheck KEV) or with a public exploit (Exploit-DB, Metasploit) is **fire** on a host when its version is **proven** there and **smoke** while the version is only inferred; a CVE that is neither is **ice**, whatever its CVSS or EPSS, proven or not. A distribution-patched match and one whose CVE has no affected version range stay ice. CVSS and EPSS stay on every row, column, sort, badge, report and in the AI's context; they no longer set heat, so a non-KEV, unexploited CVSS 9.8 finding that showed fire or smoke now shows ice. Proof is one function, `match_policy.version_proven`: hard evidence (a template hit, the distribution tracker, the host's own report, runZero, a validation hit) or the version read from the component itself (a version-class template hit, `template_result: version_only`; a JavaScript library's own file banner or code signature for the copy that runs; or a service banner nmap's probe read from a vendor-built product on an allow-list, `match_policy.vendor_built`: appliances and vendor servers such as Fortinet, Citrix, Ivanti, Jenkins, Confluence or ActiveMQ; a product not on it is no proof by its banner). "Vulnerability confirmed" (`has_hard_evidence`: what lifts triage, creates findings and counts as a validation hit) and "version proven" (what the heat rule reads) are two separate notions: a version check proves the version, not the flaw. A file name, CDN path or `?ver=` hint, technology detection, a shadowed or unresolved library copy, and the banner of any other product (what a distribution packages, such as OpenSSH, Apache httpd, nginx, OpenSSL, MySQL, PHP, Exim, BIND, Dovecot or PowerDNS, even with no distribution marker in it as on RHEL, SUSE and Amazon Linux; what the operating system services, such as IIS or Microsoft ESMTP; or a product cvENGINE does not know) without a tracker verdict are never proof. A scanner finding that names CVEs (a nuclei CVE template, the CVE template pass, OpenVAS, WPScan) follows the same rule instead of its severity: hot and confirmed is fire, hot but unconfirmed (OpenVAS below its quality bar, WPScan) smoke, not hot ice, in its heat, its share of the host score and its validation lane; a finding that names no CVE (an exposure, a misconfiguration) keeps its severity heat. Every consumer follows: lists, counts, facets, host heat and score, the map, reports (a legend for the policy and each finding's reason), notifications (a smoke match is paged once when proof turns it fire, including proof that arrives through correlation; a proven match is paged once when its CVE joins the KEV or gains an exploit; a finding is paged once per heat rule, not once per scan, and a mark under one rule never holds back a page that is new under another; ice, including a template hit on an ice CVE, never pages; the update and any later switch of the policy page nothing by themselves: migration 0139 and the workers mark what was already fire under the rule in force as announced, and leave what is still waiting for its page), the validation lane (a KEV match whose version is not proven is still validated first; an ice match, whatever its CVSS, last) and OpenVAS (a host whose only CVE matches are ice no longer qualifies). Rows carry `heat_reason` ("on the KEV; version not proven", "not on the KEV and no known exploit") in the UI, the REST API and MCP, with `version_proven` and `exploit`. Auto-fire and the CVE template pass keep testing the CVEs they always did: the policy decides heat, not detection. The first worker tick after the update queues one full host rescore. **To roll back**, set `CVENGINE_HEAT_POLICY=legacy` in `.env` (`make up` to apply) or save 0 on Settings, configMaxxing, **Heat policy**: the old classification returns at once, `CVENGINE_VERSION_ONLY_MAX_HEAT` applies again (it is not read under the new rule), and host scores follow within minutes.

### Fixed

- **A gevent worker answers remote control again after a Redis blip.** celery's gevent remote-control loop caught only a timeout, so a dropped broker connection ended it: the worker kept running tasks but answered no ping, inspect, `autoscale` or revoke until it was restarted (with kombu 5.7 the task connection reconnects in place, so nothing restarted the loop). Lost with it were the discovery concurrency resize and the auto-tune resize, the admin worker panel, revokes, and the worker answers the reaper's orphan check relies on. The loop now logs the drop and reconnects, waiting 1 s and doubling up to 30 s while the connection keeps failing (`reNgine/gevent_pidbox.py`).
- **A redelivered internal scan message runs nothing again.** `run_internal_scan` is acks_late: a message delivered again after its scan ended (an ack lost to a dropped connection, the visibility timeout, the reaper) re-ran the pipeline and sent the Sentinel tasks again. A finished scan now returns at once, as a discovery scan already did, and keeps the Celery id of the task that ran it.
- **A JavaScript library match whose served file states an affected version is confirmed, not left at Needs validation.** When cvENGINE itself read the library's file and the file's own banner or code states the version, for the copy the page runs, inside the affected range (the library clause `match_policy.version_proven` and the heat policy read), the rules set that row to **Confirmed** with a reason built from the evidence ("jQuery 2.1.3 is served at https://www.example.com/js/jquery.min.js (the file's own banner states v2.1.3), and it is the copy the page runs; ..."). The served file is the code every visitor runs, so the version-only ceiling (#210), written for banners that can hide a backported fix, does not hold it; a version from technology detection, nmap, a file name or a shadowed or unresolved copy keeps the ceiling. Only the row changes: the stored answer, the legacy `ai_verdict`, heat, paging, closure standing, the host score, grouping and the validation schedule are what they would be without the rule. The body read is recorded as cvENGINE's own check ("Library body read": the script URL, what the file states, the page that runs it and when), and How it was found says "read from the file's own banner at <URL>", "Loaded by <page URL>" and "Found by: library body read" instead of "technology fingerprint of the web server". Existing matches are brought in line once, by a background task after the upgrade (open matches only, no model call, no page). Also fixed: a library copy that was read but is shadowed by a later copy of the same version that was never read no longer counts as proof, so such a KEV match shows smoke, not fire (`evidence.copies` must show the copy at `script_url` read and running).
- **OpenVAS imports what a finished scan found (reported by camstork, #394).** On gvmd 22.7 every finished task was imported with 0 results as "OpenVAS ran but reported no scanned host", its hosts were marked failed and came back in three days, and no finding was ever imported. Two reading mistakes, found in gvmd's own source: `get_results` sends the results as direct children of its answer, but the import looked for them inside a `<results>` element (an empty marker in gvmd's answer); and the report's host list was asked without `details`, which gvmd answers with counts only. A finished task is now read from its own report (`details`, `result_hosts_only=0` so a scanned host without a result counts as scanned, `min_qod=0`, no overrides, 100 results a page), with `get_results` as the fallback for a task that names no report. Both page on until a page is empty or every result gvmd counts is read, so a gvmd whose "Max Rows Per Page" is below 100 is read to the end, and a read that ends short of gvmd's count is imported as partial and closes nothing. A report gvmd says holds results of which none could be read imports nothing and closes nothing. The task and report of an inconclusive run or a failed import are no longer deleted at once: they stay in GVM for `CVENGINE_OPENVAS_KEEP_DAYS` (7) days and the sweep removes them after, and the worker log records the result count gvmd gives before anything is removed (read in gvmd 22.x to 24.x's level names, `hole`, `warning`, `info` and `log`, and in gvmd 25's, each level once); a kept task that is already gone from gvmd is no longer asked for again. `manage.py openvas_task_report --run <n>` (or `--task <id>`) prints what gvmd has for a task the way the import reads it, read only. The fake gvmd the tests use answered in the wrong shape, which is why no test saw it; it now answers in the shapes of gvmd's printers.
- **OpenVAS activity lines say which run, host and GVM task they are about (reported by camstork, #394).** A run is one line from start to end, "OpenVAS run 12 on www.example.com (GVM task cvengine-run-12)", whose status and percentage follow GVM's at each step and which ends with how long it took and how it ended; the import line carries the same name, and an inconclusive one says how many results gvmd counts for the task and how long GVM spent on each host. The detail of each line carries the run, the hosts, their addresses and the GVM task name and id.
- **The OpenVAS login is made on gvmd 22.7 (reported by camstork, #394).** Setting up the login failed with "Failed to find user": gvmd lets only a user's owner or a Super Admin modify it, and the containers' `admin` is owned by nobody, so it cannot change its own password over GMP. cvENGINE now creates a user of its own, `cvengine` (Admin role), with the generated password when that happens, and `make openvas-user` says how to retire `admin` / `admin` inside the gvmd container and that gvmd must be restarted before the old password stops working. A login saved before is left as it is.
- **`make openvas-user` and the other scripts that reuse the running stack's Compose files work in a path with spaces (reported by camstork, #394).** `scripts/compose-layout.sh` split the Compose file list on spaces, so a project under `/Volumes/My Disk` stopped with "not a Compose file".
- **The OpenVAS add-on checks Docker's disk before it starts (reported by camstork, #394).** The first feed load needs about 60 GB (measured: the gvmd database 27.7 GB, the SCAP feed 11.4 GB, the images about 20 GB), not the 25 GB the docs said, and it filled a Docker Desktop disk to 98 % with no warning. `make openvas-up` and the switch in the app now refuse below 60 GB free (15 GB once the database volume exists) with the figures; `CVENGINE_OPENVAS_MIN_FREE_GB` sets another floor and `0` skips the check.
- **The OpenVAS memory guard reads the machine the scanner runs on (reported by camstork, #394).** It read the celery container's own memory limit (4 GB by default) and refused a scan with "3294 MB free" while the Docker VM had 12.6 GB available. It now reads the Docker host's memory (on Docker Desktop, its VM's), and skips the memory checks for a gvmd on another machine (`CVENGINE_OPENVAS_HOST`); `CVENGINE_OPENVAS_MIN_AVAILABLE_MB` still sets the floor.
- **A worker no longer loses a task slot when the broker connection drops during an acknowledgement.** With the Redis broker, kombu removes an acknowledged message from the broker before it frees the delivery in the worker's own count of unacknowledged messages; when the connection dropped in between (a Redis restart or failover, a network blip), the error was only logged and the delivery kept counting against the prefetch. For acks_late tasks (discoveries, host assessments, internal scans) the prefetch is what bounds how many run at once, so a discovery worker with a ceiling of 4 ran 3 at a time from then on, one fewer again after each such drop, until it was restarted. kombu 5.7 keeps the channel across a dropped connection, where earlier versions restarted the consumer and so hid it. The acknowledgement now tries once more on a new connection and frees the delivery whatever happens (`reNgine/broker_acks.py`); a message the broker still could not be told about comes back after the visibility timeout, as before. This is also why `test_gevent_worker_e2e`'s broker reconnect test failed now and then in CI: its cut sometimes landed on an acknowledgement.
- **One organization's host can no longer close another organization's findings, or show its notes on them.** Two stores keep an answer for every host with the same evidence, with no organization attached: the verdict correlation starts a new CVE match from (same CVE, product, version, range result and source) and the AI triage answer reused for the same evidence signature. A closing answer reached on one host was handed to the others: an automatic "different product" or operating-system closure, which reads that host's current port or technology record, and an AI answer to what that host showed (a banner, a page title, a path, a response). So a host whose owner controls what it says, in one organization, could get a matching finding of another organization started or triaged as not affected, and it stayed closed when no AI provider re-asked. Every shared answer also carried the model's words about the host it was reached on (which may quote its page title, certificate or banner) onto the other findings, their reports and the correlation table. Now a closing answer is never stored for correlation and never seeds a match (only affected and uncertain answers do); an automatic identity closure is kept on its own host only; and a stored closing triage answer is reused only in the project it was reached in (on the same host for every kind but a CVE match, whose shown text is part of its signature), never, from another host, on a finding someone is assigned to or working on or a CVE match with hard evidence (a runZero report, the host's own inventory, a validation hit). Elsewhere the finding asks its own question, within the usual triage limits, and shows needs validation with a note only while that answer is pending. A shared answer no longer starts a match with hard evidence as uncertain (which kept it off the fire page), and the model's suggested severity step down is shown only on the findings of the host and project it was reached on. Across projects an answer is shared only when it raises attention (likely, confirmed), was given by a fixed rule, or is all the finding's own evidence allows (a version string alone is needs validation at most); an AI "needs validation" from another project, or from another host for a CVE match with a runZero report, the host's own inventory or a validation hit, does not lower a finding: it asks its own question (one per project for a CVE match), and a waiting CVE match keeps its legacy verdict. Only the classification is shared: the model's reason, impact, remediation and advice are shown (in the app, the API, MCP and reports) only in the project the answer was reached in, other findings show "Assessed from a matching finding elsewhere", and the correlation table keeps no model words (migration 0140 takes them off the table and the matches it seeded, and correlation no longer copies the table's words onto a new match). One question still serves a project's hosts, and a rescan of the same host keeps its own answer. Migration `cveFeed` 0140 adds two columns to the stored answers (where each was reached; nothing is rewritten) and an index on the provider-call ledger, and fills the columns in where the ledger proves a single project (or, with no call on record, all the findings holding the answer are in one project); an answer held across organizations keeps none, closes nothing more and shows its words nowhere. The first correlation after the update reviews, once, what such answers already did. It reopens only across organizations (an instance with one organization only loses other projects' notes and the shared closing rows): a finding another organization's answer closed is held for validation and asks its own question, a finding another organization's AI answer left at needs validation asks its own question too (keeping its classification until then), a CVE match seeded closed at correlation becomes unjudged, another project's words on a finding are replaced by the fixed note, and the shared closing rows are removed. A host's own closure is kept, including an identity or operating-system closure its sighting still supports, a closure reached in its own organization, and one of unknown origin held only in its organization. It works in chunks, each in its own transaction, and writes one activity line per project with what changed there; `manage.py release_shared_closures` shows what it would change, and `--apply` runs it again by hand ([Triage and validation](docs/PIPELINE.md#triage-and-validation)).
- **Nuclei findings keep the CVE ids nuclei reports.** The parser read `classification.cve_id`, but nuclei writes `cve-id` (and `cwe-id`), so a finding of a nuclei CVE template carried no CVE id and could not follow the CVE's heat. Both spellings, a list or a comma-separated string, are read and upper-cased; a Sentinel's nuclei result may now carry `cve_ids` too. Findings stored before keep none (nuclei's raw output is not stored); the next scan's findings carry them.
- **The 1.3.2 upgrade no longer fails on an install with an agent held back by the old suspension rule.** Migration `agentapi` 0009 aborted with `cannot CREATE INDEX "agentapi_agent" because it has pending trigger events` on any database with a suspended agent carrying the old "another agent of this user is suspended or revoked" note: its backfill updated those rows in the same transaction that added the deferred `held_by` foreign key, so a key check was still pending when Django created that column's index. The backfill now checks keys as each statement ends (PostgreSQL) and updates each row once before naming the agent that held it back; what it fills in is unchanged, and so is the schema. An install that already applied 0009 is unaffected; one where it failed can run the update again.
- **Auto-tuning: a Sentinel that misses a heartbeat no longer pulls the aimed-high settings down.** A Sentinel missing from a single ten-minute measurement (a restart, a network hiccup longer than the three-minute heartbeat window) lowered every target sized from the Sentinel slots, so the discovery pool, the assessment batch, the conductor and the chunks of one stage were brought down at once and then climbed back one probe at a time, at most two a day: days to get back to where they were. A Sentinel now counts for sizing until it has been missing from the measurements for 25 minutes (three runs in a row); one that is really gone is followed as before, 20 minutes later.
- **Auto-tuning: the quick restore after an emergency back-off happens once a day.** When the database stopped answering in two runs, the discovery pool and the conductor backed off from Redis and came back in one step as soon as the database had room again. If the restored level was itself what ran the database out of connections, this repeated every hour or so. A second emergency within 24 hours of such a restore now marks the restored level as one that caused trouble: the setting stays a step under it and comes back only by the normal slow probes.
- **Auto-tuning: the emergency back-off respects pins.** A discovery pool or conductor pinned by a value saved on configMaxxing (or by an environment variable other than its default) after the tuner had moved it could still be backed off while the database was away, and the discovery workers were resized below the pinned number until the database came back. The emergency back-off now reads the pins the way the workers do (the saved values' Redis copy and the environment) and leaves a pinned setting alone.
- **The OpenVAS add-on can be pulled again.** Greenbone removed the `openvas-scanner` image digest `docker-compose.yml` pinned (`sha256:5067aa68...`; the registry answers 404), so a fresh `docker compose --profile openvas pull` failed. Both services that run it now pin the current `stable` digest, `sha256:7a7c440cf675a83ca1b6d8284eb28450893a8b05f5fed1f98e997df605792632` (the one the Sentinel client pins), and docs/OPENVAS.md lists it. The other pinned Greenbone digests still resolve and are unchanged.
- **`CVENGINE_OPENVAS_MAX_HOSTS` is held to 16.** It had no upper bound, and a Sentinel refuses an OpenVAS task of more than 16 hosts, so a server set higher handed Sentinels tasks that always failed. A larger value is now used as 16, with a warning in the log, and a task sent to a Sentinel never names more than 16 hosts.
- **A nuclei pass sent to a Sentinel with no severities runs the same severities as here.** The task carried an empty `severities` list, which the client reads as every severity, `unknown` included; it now carries the server's own default, info to critical (`nuclei_scan.SEVERITIES`), so a Sentinel run and a local run of the same pass agree.
- **A JavaScript library version a technology scan named is checked against the host's own page, for every library cvENGINE reads.** The 1.3.2 re-cut read library versions from the files themselves, but a version only technology detection named (httpx and Wappalyzer, the nuclei technology pass, bbot: source `scan`) bypassed it, uncapped at confidence 0.85 to 0.95. On one live WordPress 7.1 site the only jQuery core is `jquery.min.js?ver=3.7.1` (its file says 3.7.1), yet the theme's asset cache-buster `?ver=1.0.4`, a forms plugin's `?ver=3.2.1` and jQuery Migrate's 3.4.1 were matched as jQuery 1.0.4, 3.2.1 and 3.4.1 against CVE-2020-11022 and CVE-2020-11023. Such a version is now a candidate at the file-name confidence (0.4, `version_source: other`), never hard evidence, and the page's own files never close it. **File evidence informs and caps; only a redirect alias closes.** Static reading cannot prove that a page loads nothing else at run time (a `document.write`, a script tag appended by jQuery, a `fetch` followed by `eval`, `createElementNS`, a contextual fragment and so on), so even when every script of the front page, on every web port, was read whole, its files state another version and no loader was recognised, the match stays open, with a note saying what the file states ("the script named 1.12.4 states jQuery 3.7.1 in its own file; the version the browser scan reported may not be the one the page runs", or "the page's own scripts state jQuery 3.7.1; the 1.0.4 the technology scan reported may not be the one the page runs"). Under the KEV heat policy an inferred version is smoke at most, so such a match never pages. What the read finds still shapes the note: whether every script of the page was read whole (a script not served, refused, over the read budget of six a host, on a third-party host, cut at the read limit, carrying a second library or a second copy anywhere in it, or loading another script at run time, or inline code not read, is not), whether the page explains the version (a script URL carries it, or another library on the page states it), and whether the library is one whose code is recognised wherever it appears (jQuery, jQuery UI and jQuery Migrate: `js_libraries.CLOSABLE`; any other library, Moment.js, Vue.js, DataTables, Bootstrap, may sit unrecognised in a bundle or inline, and its note says so). A file the read recognises nothing in (a minified build without its banner) proves nothing. The whole-body search (`whole_body`) and the loader rule (`runtime_loaders`) are shared with the validation's script read below, which likewise never closes on a fixed version. The page counts only when the front page of every web port of the host was read lately (8 days), whole (not cut, within 80 scripts, 2,000 script tags and 100 inline scripts) and is the site itself (not a login, a redirect or maintenance stub, a challenge, or a page with no script); the rule reads the front page only, never the host's other pages. The only closure is the redirect alias (see the next entry). Library matches earlier builds closed on the page's files, an absence or a cache-buster (`named_by_script`, `contradicted_by_file`, `file_states_nothing`, `no_such_script`, `cache_buster`), and validation's fixed-version script-read closures, are reopened once at the first correlation after the upgrade (`library_reopen.reopen_once`, marker `library_file_closures_133`): only closures the correlation made automatically on a match nobody worked, never a redirect alias or a person's closure; the match goes back to unjudged at confidence 0.4 and its triage row to pending, nothing is paged, and each project gets one activity line with its own count. The rule covers every library in the registry (`js_libraries.LIBRARIES`: jQuery, jQuery UI and Migrate, Bootstrap, AngularJS, React, Vue.js, Lodash, Moment.js, Underscore.js, Swiper, DataTables); other products are unchanged. The route check now reads a front page up to 300 KB (other routes stay at 64 KB) and the scripts of a front page that answers 404 or 410 (a hosted landing site whose `/` is missing still loads its scripts: on the same live project a real jQuery 1.11.2 stays matched to CVE-2020-11023, proven by its file's banner), and records which front pages it parsed, whether they were cut, how many of their scripts were not read whole, the run-time loaders of the page itself and the versions its script URLs carry (`script_page` in the route evidence, kept as data).
- **A host that only redirects no longer inherits the libraries of the page it redirects to.** Five names of one live site that answer a 301 to it carried its jQuery and Bootstrap matches: the version probes follow a redirect within the host's group and recorded the target page's scripts, `Server` header and generator tags on the redirecting host, and correlation matched them, together with the libraries other tools saw after following the redirect. For a host that redirected, the probes now read only its own redirect answer (its headers), and correlation matches no library version on a host whose every front page answers a permanent redirect (301 or 308) and leads to another host (reason: "the host only redirects to example.com"); a temporary redirect (302, 303, 307: a login hop, a geo or maintenance switch) makes no alias, and neither does a redirect to a host the project does not inventory (the CVE would silently leave the project: the match stays open). A redirect to the host itself (http to https, `/` to `/en/`) changes nothing, and a host with any other answer at a front page (a page, a 404 page, a 403), or with a web port whose front page the route check has not read (an application on 8443 beside a redirecting 443), is not treated as an alias. Closed matches reopen if the host serves a page of its own again; one a person worked is kept.
- **Comparing a route with a missing page no longer stalls on a hostile page.** The soft-404 normalisation took a CSRF `<meta>` token out with a pattern that backtracked over every `<meta` (16 s of CPU on 16 KB of repeated `<meta name="csrf `, 40 s on 300 KB of `<meta `). It now finds each `<meta>` tag first and looks inside that tag only, in linear time, and a front page, which is never compared, is not normalised at all.
- **A template that only matches a version string no longer confirms a CVE.** The AI template writer may detect a CVE "by version" and the safety gate accepts a version regex, so a template that matched `jQuery v3.4.1` in a script set the match's template result to vulnerable: that counted as hard evidence, lifted a version-only KEV match to Fire, recorded a confirmed nuclei-cve finding and paged it as a confirmed template hit, which never looked at heat. Not seen on live data, but any product could reach it. Each template is now classified deterministically, as an allowlist that defaults to a real check: a template is a version check only when it has one request block that only reads (GET or HEAD of a static path, no query, payload, body, empty segment or exposure page such as a status page (`/status`, `nginx_status`, `fpm-status`), a configuration, backup, `.env`, `config.json`, `.npmrc`, `.aws/credentials`, `id_rsa`, `.htpasswd`, `swagger.json` or `WEB-INF/web.xml` file, an error log, a CGI script, a registry catalogue (`/v2/_catalog`), or a metrics, environment, Jolokia or trace endpoint, and no request header but `Host: {{Hostname}}`; or a network banner read with nothing sent), and its matchers decide on a version (`compare_versions` over a regex extractor whose group captures only digits and dots, a regex that spells a version number, or product-and-version words, never an HTTP status line in any form, `HTTP/1.1 200` or a regex such as `^HTTP.1\.0 200`, `HTTP\S1\.1` or `HTTP\x2F1\.1`), the others only naming the product with words from the product part of the template's name (before its first ` - `: the vulnerability title after it, such as "Email Address Disclosure", names no product), its metadata vendor or product or its CPE's product, matching a MIME type or wanting a 2xx status. Flow, ssl, dns and any other block, any unusual header, matcher or key, and anything unreadable or malformed make it a real check. A version check's hit is recorded as `version_only` on the match (and as a re-affirmed version in validation), is not hard evidence, keeps the match version-only, records no nuclei-cve finding and pages nothing. Only a `vulnerable` result ever replaces a `vulnerable` one: a version hit, a miss or an error of a later run never lowers a confirmation from nuclei, OpenVAS or testssl. `notify.template_hits` also leaves out any pair a version check matched whose matches in the same project are still version-only, an auto-fire run's single message included (each hit is checked against the project it was tested for). Over the current community CVE templates (nuclei-templates, 4,510 under `http/cves` and `network/cves`), 4 classify as version checks (EyesOfNetwork's stylesheet, Pega Infinity's login page, the Elementor readme, Zimbra's `ZmSettings.js`, each a `compare_versions` over a version the page states, gated by the product's name); those 4 stop producing Fire and pages, which is intended. Every other template, version comparisons with a less strict shape included, stays a confirmed hit as before. Results recorded before this release are not rewritten.
- **A jQuery (or other client-side library) CVE is checked by reading the page's scripts, not by nmap.** The shared detection record of CVE-2020-11022, CVE-2020-11023 and every other CVE in a library cvENGINE reads from script files (jQuery, jQuery UI, jQuery Migrate, Bootstrap, AngularJS, React, Vue.js, Lodash, Moment.js, Underscore.js, Swiper, DataTables) told people and the validation to run `nmap -sV`, which cannot read a version out of a script, and to check OS packages named `jquery`, `jquery-rails` and `components/jquery`, which print UNKNOWN on every host (Debian's package is `libjs-jquery`). On the owner's instance the Fire lane ran nmap and a HEAD for 26 known-exploited jQuery matches and could never decide one. The rules now write a `script_version` check (the version read from the file itself by the `html-libraries` read, which copy runs, never a `?ver=` string or the file name alone; a hit proves the version is on the page, not that the page is exploitable), recommend the `html-libraries probe` instead of nmap, check on the host only the system-wide packages that ship the library (`libjs-jquery`, `node-jquery`, `js-jquery`, `libjs-jquery-ui`, `js-jquery-ui`, Bootstrap's packages per release line, and the like) and say that a copy a website bundles is not a package. Validation of such a match reads every web base's front page and its scripts again (`script_read`) under every existing gate (passive and manual only targets, a target set to one host, exclusions, the bug bounty scope and, for an automatic run, the project's confirmation of automated testing, which an automatic validation request now checks for every CVE match; script budgets; an internal host is never fetched by the server and is left as it was, with the reason), once per host for ten minutes however many library CVEs it has. A version still in range and read from the file is re-affirmed with the fresh version evidence, never a hit and never hard evidence. A match is closed only on what is truly false, through the guarded closure (`Closed automatically: fresh script read`, this host only), never one a person worked or other evidence vouches for, in one case only: every root of the host permanently (301 or 308) redirects to another host that is one of the project's own inventoried hosts, for a match made from a script of the front page (a version another tool named may be on a deeper route the host still serves, and stays open). A fixed version never closes a match (1.3.3, final review): even when the match's own file states one fixed version, every web base and every script with a source on every page was read whole, searched end to end and shows no loader, a page can still load another copy at run time that no static read sees, so the read is `observed`, with what the file states. An absence never closes: the match's own script gone (404), a library the page no longer shows, moved into a bundle or behind a loader, is left to the next scan's ordinary stale closure. A temporary redirect, an unread, failing (5xx), held or cut-short file, a spent budget and a failed read change nothing either. A person's own Validate now runs in a bug bounty project that has not confirmed automated testing (an automatic one still does not) and always reads the page again; on a match a script read closed it reads again instead of returning the old closure, and a read that finds an affected copy again (the one that runs, or any copy or file of the page) reopens it. Every hop of the read's own requests, redirects included, passes the bug bounty scope, the one-host target rule and a host-scoped target's own host, besides the address, exclusion and archive gate; a port this server's egress governor holds is recorded as `held`. An advisory about React's or Vue's server-side code (React Server Components and `react-server-dom-*`, `react-dom/server`, Next.js, `vue-server-renderer`, server-side rendering: CVE-2025-55182) is not taken for the browser library: its record keeps its ordinary checks and the one-time rewrite leaves it alone. Existing records are rewritten once by the detection task with no manual step (a model-written record keeps its own checks and words and is queued for the model's own revision; a manual one is left alone). The match page and the AI prompts quote how the version was read. Records of every other CVE are unchanged.

### Security

- **The web screenshot browser runs inside Chromium's sandbox, and refuses to run without it.** The browser renders pages from scanned, often hostile hosts; it ran without its own sandbox (Playwright's default), so a renderer exploit ran native code as `nobody` in the celery container, with its network (Redis, Postgres, the other containers, the cloud metadata address). It now launches with `chromium_sandbox=True` under Playwright's seccomp profile (see Upgrade notes): each renderer runs in its own user and PID namespace under Chromium's seccomp filter, with no network or file system of its own. Every batch checks the sandbox engaged before it opens a page (the renderer's namespaces and seccomp filters, and no `--no-sandbox`), and so does the celery container at every start; when it cannot, screenshots turn off with the reason rather than run unsandboxed. `CVENGINE_SCREENSHOT_SANDBOX=0` is the explicit, unsafe way back. CI runs the built image with and without the profile to prove both. Also: the scan results volume (scan output, PDF reports, log archives) is no longer readable by `nobody`, and `scripts/check_tool_versions.py` shows the Chromium build Playwright bundles next to the current Chrome stable every week (a warning, never a release gate: Chromium lags Chrome stable between Playwright releases, and only a Playwright bump and an image rebuild move it). What the browser process can still reach is set out in docs/PIPELINE.md ("Web screenshots"); taking it off the network needs a container of its own (planned for 1.4).
- **Pillow's lowest allowed version is now 13.0.0 (was 10).** `web/requirements.txt` gives Pillow as a range, `>=10,<14`, so an environment that already had Pillow 10 to 12 kept it on `pip install -r`, and every release before 13.0.0 is affected by the Pillow advisories published in October 2026 (among them GHSA-8g5g-8vmr-9782, GHSA-332p-7hrc-92wf and GHSA-5x8w-8jj5-q52h, critical heap buffer overflows in the JPEG, TIFF and FlashPix decoders, and GHSA-xvh5-g6gv-559v and GHSA-78g4-xqwv-q3g8, high). The range is now `>=13.0.0,<14`. A fresh image build already installed 13.0.0, the newest release under the cap, so a rebuilt image is unchanged; the floor protects other installs. Pillow opens the web and RDP screenshots (the comparison hash and the blank-frame check). Dependabot cannot report on Pillow because the line is a range, not a pin. The three high Dependabot alerts on `master` (GHSA-mfr5-77xh-9j7h, GHSA-cggf-277g-3q54 and GHSA-5849-834g-hvw5, in pypdf 6.21.3) were already fixed in 1.3.2 by pypdf 6.21.4.

## v1.3.2 (2026-11-01)

### Upgrade notes

- **Auto-tuning is on by default.** After this update cvENGINE tunes the throughput settings of Settings, configMaxxing itself (mode Auto). What stays as you set it (pinned, never moved): every value you saved on configMaxxing before the update (the row says "Pinned by <you>"; "Let auto-tune manage this" hands it over), and every one of these settings you set in `.env` to something other than its built-in default, for example `DISCOVERY_CONCURRENCY=4` or `HEAVY_MAX_NMAP=1` (the row says "Pinned by the environment (NAME=value)"; remove it from `.env`, or save a value on the page, to change that). A value in `.env` equal to the default is not a pin (the compose files pass `DISCOVERY_CONCURRENCY=8` when nothing is set). Everything else jumps once: about ten minutes after the update, when memory and the Sentinels have reported (at the latest after 30 minutes), the unpinned settings go up to what this machine sensibly carries, never below their defaults unless the memory budget or the database says so (the page says what it was sized for), then back off at once on trouble and probe back up slowly; watch the Tuning history after the update. To tune by hand, choose Off under Settings, configMaxxing, Auto-tuning (or Suggest to see recommendations only); to turn it off for the whole instance, add `CVENGINE_AUTOTUNE=0` to `.env` and run `make up`. The three-hour AI review no longer moves scanner limits. The AI advisor, when an AI provider is set up, uses at most 8 small calls a day (`CVENGINE_AUTOTUNE_AI_MAX_PER_DAY`, under AI spend).
- **Reply limits for local models and Anthropic.** `CVENGINE_AI_MAX_TOKENS` now applies to Anthropic as well, and vLLM, LM Studio and Ollama are sent 2048 tokens by default (400 for a finding triage) where they were sent none. A reasoning model that thinks before it answers can now end at that limit with no answer: raise `CVENGINE_AI_MAX_TOKENS` or turn its reasoning off ([Local models](docs/DEPLOYMENT.md#local-models), [CONFIGURATION.md](docs/CONFIGURATION.md#ai-requests)).
- **bbot no longer live-verifies the credentials it finds.** Its `trufflehog` module tests a found secret against the issuer's live API, so a third party's credential found on a target was used with no gate. The module is now off in every bbot mode. `CVENGINE_BBOT_TRUFFLEHOG=1` turns it back on for a deep discovery of a project with no bug bounty engagement; a bug bounty project never runs it, whatever the setting. Secrets found during scans are also masked before they reach the AI provider (stored findings, the app and reports are unchanged).
- **Purge the certificate lookup queue once.** Messages already in `ctwatch_queue` carry no expiry or marker, so the new bound (`CVENGINE_CT_BACKLOG_MAX`, default 500) does not shrink an old backlog. After the update, run in the celery container `celery -A reNgine.tasks purge -Q ctwatch_queue -f`, and once more after a worker restart (messages a worker holds for their ETA return to the queue then). Nothing is lost: the sweep re-derives its work from the `ct-watch` ledger.
- **Fourteen additive migrations, no manual step:** `cveFeed` 0129 (two columns on the feed watermark), 0130 (alias and note on a host's name record), 0131 (the scan concurrency table), 0132 (the table behind the other configMaxxing settings), 0133 (the screenshot table), 0134 (the schedule of the hourly screenshot backfill) 0135 (the auto-tuning mode and history tables and the schedule of the ten-minute tuning run; it only records who saved each existing configMaxxing value) and 0136 (one nullable column on a CVE match that records when it was paged as Fire; nothing is rewritten), `dashboard` 0031 (the AI request settings, all nullable or empty), `targetApp` 0006 (the discovery mode of a target; every existing target gets standard, so nothing changes for targets added before) and 0007 (alias and note on a target), `agentapi` 0008 (clears the automatic suspension note from agents that are already active) and 0009 (when an agent's status last changed, and the cause of a hold), and `startScan` 0025 (a scan's discovery mode, empty default). New columns have empty or standard defaults and the new tables are empty until a value is saved.
- **The Live screen is refreshed in the background, on a new queue.** A celery beat entry (`cvengine-live-refresh`) recomputes the snapshots of the scopes people are looking at on a new `live_cache_queue`, which the celery container's classic worker already consumes (still four workers, no new container). It is small and skips itself when that worker is busy, but if the classic worker (notifications, WPScan and the other sweeps) looks starved, **set `CVENGINE_LIVE_REFRESH=0` first**: the refresher stops and every Live poll is computed by the request that finds its snapshot stale, as before. The lists of projects a user may see are also cached for up to 15 seconds for the four Live polls (`CVENGINE_AUTH_CACHE_SECONDS`, `0` is off); a change made through the application takes effect on the next poll.
- **The image now contains a browser, for web screenshots.** Playwright's headless Chromium adds about 300 MB to the image: rebuild it (`make up` and the updater do) or screenshots stay off with a note on the host page. A new `screenshot_queue` is consumed by the existing classic worker (still four workers). `CVENGINE_SCREENSHOTS=0` turns the whole feature off (default on); `CVENGINE_SCREENSHOT_TTL_HOURS` (72), `_RETRY_HOURS` (24), `_MAX_PER_SCAN` (50), `_BACKFILL_PER_PROJECT` (20), `_BACKFILL_MAX` (100), `_TIMEOUT` (20 s), `_MAX_BYTES` (2 MiB), `_PROJECT_MB` (500, the storage one project may use for pictures), `_MAX_RSS_MB` (1500, the memory the browser and everything it started may hold) and `_USER` (`nobody`) tune it; see [CONFIGURATION.md](docs/CONFIGURATION.md). The hourly backfill photographs hosts that have no picture yet, 20 per project and 100 per run, so pictures appear over the first hours after the update.
- **The update recreates the `db` container once, and the workers now really run as many tasks as they are set to.** PostgreSQL's `max_connections` is now 300 (`POSTGRES_MAX_CONNECTIONS` in `.env` overrides it), which changes the `db` service's definition, so the update recreates it once before the migration (a restart of a few seconds); later updates leave it running. Workers used to run one task at a time under a gevent autoscaler bug, so a Sentinel could sit idle behind one long task; they now run up to their ceiling, and each running task holds a database connection. The classic worker's default ceiling is 10 (`CLASSIC_CONCURRENCY`, was 20). If you scan many hosts, raise **Hosts assessed at once** (Settings, configMaxxing, Scan concurrency) to 16 to 24 so Sentinels stay busy.
- **Agents held back by the old rule need one reinstatement.** An App Admin's new agents are no longer held back. An agent that is still suspended by the old rule must be reinstated once (or deleted, if revoked). `CVENGINE_AGENT_HOLD_DAYS=0` keeps the old no-time-limit hold for everyone except App Admins.
- **New optional settings.** Every default keeps the old behaviour or is the new bound described in its entry. In the environment: `CVENGINE_STAGE_PARALLEL` (4; `1` is one chunk at a time), `CVENGINE_ASSESS_BATCH` (8; `1` is one host per run), `CVENGINE_VALIDATE_MIN_GAP_HOURS` (12; `0` off), `CVENGINE_HYGIENE_CLEARTEXT` (on), `CVENGINE_AI_REASONING` and `CVENGINE_AI_CONNECT_TIMEOUT` (unset sends every AI request as before), `CVENGINE_CT_BACKLOG_MAX` (500), `CVENGINE_TTL_CT_WATCH_FAILED` (6 hours), `CVENGINE_BBOT_TRUFFLEHOG` (off), `CVENGINE_BULK_DISCOVER_NOW` (50), `CVENGINE_DEEP_SWEEP_PER_PROJECT` (10) `CVENGINE_RANGE_REPAIR_PER_RUN` (200; `0` off), `CVENGINE_AGENT_HOLD_DAYS` (30; `0` means no time limit), `POSTGRES_MAX_CONNECTIONS` (300), `CLASSIC_CONCURRENCY` (default now 10, it was 20) and, for the Live screen cache, `CVENGINE_LIVE_REFRESH` (on; `0` turns the background refresher off), `CVENGINE_LIVE_REFRESH_SECONDS` (5, 2 to 60), `CVENGINE_LIVE_HOT_SECONDS` (60, 15 to 600) and `CVENGINE_AUTH_CACHE_SECONDS` (15, 0 to 30; `0` is off); see [CONFIGURATION.md](docs/CONFIGURATION.md). Settings, configMaxxing (App Admins only) holds the scan concurrency limits, the AI spend caps and the scan pacing numbers, and Settings, AI model holds the AI request settings; a value saved there wins over the environment, and an unset one follows it. The proxy configuration gains gzip, which the updater applies when it recreates the proxy.
- **A version-only CVE match now shows Smoke, never Fire.** Existing matches are re-rated as soon as they are read: a version-only KEV match that showed Fire now shows Smoke, while one with a template or validation hit stays Fire. Fire counts on dashboards and in reports drop by the number of version-only matches, and stored host scores follow at the next daily rescore, within a day. Nothing is paged at the upgrade. To keep the old rating, set `CVENGINE_VERSION_ONLY_MAX_HEAT=fire` in `.env` and restart (`ice` is stricter); changing it after the first start forces one full rescore at the next worker start.
- **JavaScript library matches are re-read from the scripts themselves.** A library match made from a script name or a `?ver=` string (the source of the jQuery 1.0.4 and Bootstrap 1.0.4 reports) drops to confidence 0.4 with a note at the next correlation, and none is closed for that alone. At the next scan each such host's front page is checked again, inside its 7-day window, so the script files are read. New settings: `CVENGINE_SCRIPT_BODY_READ` (default on, `0` turns script reading off), `CVENGINE_SCRIPT_READ_MAX_REQUESTS` (300) and `CVENGINE_SCRIPT_READ_MAX_SECONDS` (600). The list of shared hosting platforms gains onrender.com, blogspot.com, wixsite.com, squarespace.com, webflow.io, fly.dev, ngrok, trycloudflare.com, r2.dev, storage.googleapis.com and others; an existing target under one of these with scope mode `auto` becomes host-scoped, so it is no longer widened to the provider's domain.

### Added

- **Findings: the same JavaScript library file on sibling subdomains is one finding.** One jQuery or Bootstrap file on www., blog. and careers. of a site used to show as one CVE finding per host. The Findings list now shows it as one row, with the number of hosts, each host's own status and a host list that opens each one. **Show every host** lists them apart. Only client-side JavaScript libraries are grouped; web servers, CMSs and other server-side software stay one row per host. Every host keeps its own finding, status, validation and audit. A status change on a group changes only its open and in-progress hosts, unless closed ones are included on purpose. A host with its own proof (a template or validation hit, a confirmed classification) stays its own row. REST/MCP `list_findings` gains `group=cve`; the default is unchanged. The dashboard's CVE matches count stays per host and shows the grouped number beside it.
- **Auto-tuning of configMaxxing, on by default: aim high, then scale back.** About ten minutes after each restart or update, once memory and the Sentinels have reported, cvENGINE raises the throughput settings nobody pinned (scanner kinds at once, hosts assessed at once, hosts per assessment batch, discovery scans kept running, chunks of one stage at once, the rescan and Sentinel refresh gaps) to what this machine sensibly carries (never below what a fresh install runs, unless the memory budget or the database says so), sized from its container CPU limit (shared across the scanner kinds), the scanning container's memory budget against each scanner's real peak, the Postgres connections in use, free disk and the Sentinels' slots (Settings shows "sized for this machine"). Every ten minutes it then backs a setting off at once on evidence (memory kills, time-outs, a busy database, a silent worker, Sentinel waits that ran locally), brings a setting above its current target down at once (for example when Sentinels leave), and probes back up one step at a time after two healthy hours, never to a level that caused trouble in the last day, never while paused or updating; when the database is out of connections in two runs in a row it backs the discovery pool off one step from Redis alone and restores it once the database has room again. Modes Auto (default), Suggest (recommendations with Apply) and Off. A value saved on the page, or an environment variable set to anything other than its default, pins its setting and is never moved. Every change has a reason, a history row, an audit entry and a Revert. An optional AI advisor gives a second opinion every few hours from numbers only, inside the controller's range, with its own small daily budget (`CVENGINE_AUTOTUNE_AI_MAX_PER_DAY`). REST `/api/v1/tuning` and MCP `get_tuning_status`, `list_tuning_decisions`, `set_tuning_mode`, `apply_tuning_suggestion`, `revert_tuning_decision`, `release_tuning_pin`; `get_system_status` shows the mode, what the machine was sized for, the last run and why each setting was not raised. Kill switch: `CVENGINE_AUTOTUNE=0`.
- **CMS versions are read where the CMS states them.** One registry (`cveFeed/cms.py`) says where each product writes its version and how a page names it. WordPress core is read from the core's own assets over the whole page, the login page and `/feed/`; Joomla, Magento, TYPO3 and Umbraco are named from front-page markers (Drupal's release from its core scripts, Joomla's from its language pack and component manifest, Magento's from the admin footer and Magento 1's release notes); MyBB, Simple Machines Forum, PunBB, XMB, YetAnotherForum.NET, NodeBB and Vanilla state their release in a footer link, a script variable or a header; phpBB, XenForo and Flarum are named. A library's own version (jQuery, jquery-migrate) is never a product's. A served `wp-content/upgrade/` (the directory the October 2026 update-window RCE uses) is recorded as a finding. The places to look follow CMSeeK's version routines (credited in `THIRD-PARTY.md`).
- **A WordPress site WPScan could not reach in time is queued as its own job.** A site over the per-scan limit, out of time or out of memory used to wait for the next scan of its target. It is now queued as a `wpscan_quick` job that runs the same stage for those hosts and asks again a little later (up to 6 times); a scan a person stopped queues nothing.
- **A project with more than 500 hosts is drawn as one cluster on the dashboard graph, and you can dive into it.** The cluster replaces thousands of dots, shows the project's real total (not what the capped graph payload drew), and its hosts with findings stay visible. Double-click it, or press **Open the cloud**, and the project is drawn host by host with a bar to go back (**All projects**, or Escape). A project of 400 drawn of 900 is a cluster too. The look of the clusters is described under Changed (constellations).
- **Search by product and version.** `wordpress 7.1` finds the product at that release (7.1 and 7.1.4, never 7.10); a version alone keeps its old meaning.
- **The CVE List's "affected by default, except these ranges" records are imported** (Jenkins CVE-2024-23897): the affected releases are the gaps between the unaffected intervals, and a shape that cannot be written exactly yields nothing rather than a guess. The import counts and logs these records.
- **Stored version ranges are repaired in the background.** An hourly step of the threat-intel run (`CVENGINE_RANGE_REPAIR_PER_RUN`, 200 records by default, `0` turns it off) walks the stored CVEs and re-derives the ranges an older conversion got wrong: entries imported from the CVE List before the current conversion, blocks with no range, and cve.now's "unspecified", wildcard and comma shapes (the last are rewritten locally, with no request). A changed entry is correlated again. `manage.py repair_cve_ranges [--limit --passes --reset]` runs it by hand.
- **Scan concurrency is a setting: Settings, configMaxxing, Scan concurrency.** A user running cvENGINE locally asked how to scan more than one target at a time; the only controls were environment variables (`HEAVY_MAX_<KIND>`, `DISCOVERY_CONCURRENCY`, `CVENGINE_WORK_DEPTH`) and the web interface showed none. Settings has a new **configMaxxing** section for advanced tunables (App Admins only, collapsed until opened and remembered per person, at `/cve/settings/#configmaxxing`, with a warning that changing these can make scans slower, use more memory or send more traffic to your targets), built as a small registry of cards so more of the environment-only settings can move there later; Scan concurrency is its first card. It has a number for each local scanner kind (nmap, nuclei, bbot deep, standard and passive, naabu, route probes and the rest, 1 to 8, with a lower ceiling for the memory-hungry kinds), **Hosts assessed at once** (`DISCOVERY_CONCURRENCY`, 1 to 32) and **Hosts per assessment batch** (`CVENGINE_ASSESS_BATCH`, 1 to 64) and **Discovery scans kept running** (`CVENGINE_WORK_DEPTH`, 0 to 32). Each shows its default, the allowed range, the value from the environment if one is set, who saved it and when, and has **Reset to default**; **Reset all configMaxxing settings to defaults** resets the section. The page shows the scanning container's memory budget (`CELERY_MEM_LIMIT`) and warns when the chosen limits could want more than twice of it; the memory gate still holds work back either way. A saved value wins over the environment, which wins over the built-in default. The scanner limits and the conductor's depth apply within seconds with no restart (saved in the database, published to Redis, cached 5 seconds in each worker; without Redis the environment and defaults apply as before). The discovery pool is resized live with celery's `autoscale` remote control and the page shows the ceiling the worker reports (an autoscaler ceiling on a gevent pool, not an exact cap), or says that it has not confirmed the change and the number takes effect after the next restart or update; `celery-entrypoint.sh` reads the saved value at every start. The three-hour AI review stays inside the saved number (it becomes the top of the tuning range unless `HEAVY_TUNE_MAX_<KIND>` gives more room), and saving a number for a kind clears the review's last step for it. Every change and reset is audited (`scan_concurrency.set`, `scan_concurrency.reset`, `config_maxxing.reset_all`). It is a session-only settings API (`/cve/api/settings/scan_concurrency`) and is not part of the agent REST API or MCP. Migration `cveFeed` 0131 adds the table; nothing changes until a value is saved. New guide: [Scan more targets at once](docs/DEPLOYMENT.md#scan-more-targets-at-once), with safe values by machine size.
- **An alias and a note on targets and hosts.** On a network where most hosts have only an address, every list, search result and report showed bare IPs. A target and a host can now carry an **alias** (a short display name, one line, up to 60 characters) and a **note** (free text, up to 1000), set from **Alias** on a target row of the Targets page, from the host's **Asset info** on the Hosts page, over REST (`POST` or `PATCH .../targets/{id}/profile`, and `alias` and `note` on `.../hosts/{host}/profile`) and over MCP (`set_target_profile`, and `alias` and `note` on `set_host_profile`), with the same checks as the other writes (a role above audit, a token that is not read only) and an audit entry for each change. The alias is shown beside the address, never instead of it, in the Targets and Hosts lists, the host page, the gallery, the CSV export, the Ctrl+K search, the graph's tooltips and cards, and the PDF reports (the report page also prints the note); the address and the names a scan discovers are unchanged. The Hosts search, the target list and the all-projects search match them: a bare word and `host:` read the alias, and `alias:` and `note:` filter on them. A host's alias lives with the project's record of the host name, so it survives rescans, and when several names on the same addresses are one host, the alias of any of them shows; a host that is its own target (an address added as a target) falls back to the target's alias. Migrations `cveFeed` 0130 and `targetApp` 0007 add the four columns with an empty default. A host or target without an alias is unchanged.
- **The hygiene check now flags cleartext administration services (#282).** A MikroTik with Telnet (23) and FTP (21) open read "0 certificates, 0 issues", because hygiene covered only TLS and mail DNS. It now reports, from the ports the scans already stored and with no probe of its own: Telnet (`cleartext_telnet`), FTP when nmap did not read a certificate over AUTH TLS (`cleartext_ftp`, worded "unencrypted unless TLS is enforced", since a scan cannot prove a server refuses plain logins), rexec, rlogin and rsh on 512 to 514 (`cleartext_rservices`), and a login page the route check saw over `http://` on a host with no HTTPS service (`cleartext_http_login`). Telnet, FTP and the r-services are medium on an internal host and high on a public one (judged from the address, as the host score does); a login over plain HTTP is low and medium. One finding per host and check names every port and address; closed, filtered and unanswered ports, TLS tunnels (`telnets`, `ftps`), excluded and archived hosts, CDN edges and tailnet addresses (the Tailscale check already covers those) are never findings. They are ordinary hygiene findings: remediation status, assignee, comments, triage, reports (a "Cleartext services" group), the host score, the Hygiene page (a new Area filter), `list_hygiene_findings` (`check=cleartext`) and `GET projects/{slug}/hygiene` list them like the others, and they appear at the next hygiene run. `CVENGINE_HYGIENE_CLEARTEXT=0` turns the check off (default on): none is made and the open ones resolve at the next run. Not included: SNMP v1 and v2c in general, which needs an SNMP request the stored data does not hold (the UDP sweep already reports the default `public` community). No migration.
- **An opt-in reasoning control: `CVENGINE_AI_REASONING` (`off`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `max`).** Unset, which is the default, every request is byte for byte what it was. Set, it is applied the way each provider takes it: `reasoning_effort` for OpenAI's o-series and gpt-5 models; adaptive thinking with an effort (`thinking: {"type": "adaptive"}`, `output_config.effort`, never `budget_tokens`, never a temperature, top_p or top_k) for Claude 4.6 and later; `thinking` with a `budget_tokens` of at least 1024 and below the reply limit for Claude 3.7 to 4.5; a top-level `think` for Ollama's qwen3, deepseek-r1, gpt-oss and magistral models. vLLM, LM Studio and OpenRouter are sent nothing (set it on the server). A model that has no such control, or a level that is not valid for it (`off` for a Claude model that cannot disable thinking), ignores the setting and says so once in the log. Thinking is paid from the reply limit: raise `CVENGINE_AI_MAX_TOKENS` with it. Configuration and Deployment document each mapping. It is also a setting under Settings, AI model, Request settings, where a saved value wins over the variable.
- **A Base URL for Anthropic and OpenAI in Settings, AI model.** The field was hidden for both although the code honoured it. Optional, empty uses the vendor's own address; set it for a gateway or proxy (LiteLLM and others), which then receives the key. It is checked like the other providers' (http or https, no link-local or cloud metadata address). For Anthropic a bare address or one ending in `/v1` is the server (the messages endpoint is added); an address with another path is used as written, as before, and `localhost` is mapped to the Docker host as for the local servers. Choosing another provider in the form no longer carries the previous provider's address along.
- **One capability table for the AI providers** (`reNgine/ai_profiles.py`: label, transport, default address, key and URL needs, reply limits, which stages may pin temperature and seed, the structured-output steps, the reasoning mechanism by model name). The provider lists, the model choices and the reply-limit defaults are derived from it, and a test holds them to what they were. No request changes.
- **The chunks of one scan stage now run in parallel across Sentinel slots (`CVENGINE_STAGE_PARALLEL`, default 4).** Even with hosts batched, a stage sent its chunks to a Sentinel one at a time (`wrap_nuclei_stream`, the naabu runner and `try_nmap` each looped over 64-address chunks, and the nuclei technology pass first split its targets 100 at a time), so one scan kept ONE Sentinel slot busy: on a project with several slots, or two Sentinels, most slots idled while a 66-host scan walked a chain of about 40 two-to-four-minute tasks. `agentapi.sentinel_dispatch.dispatch_many` now creates a window of chunk tasks up front and waits for all of them in one poll loop, applying each result as it lands and refilling the window as chunks finish. The window is the smallest of the chunks left, the slots the stage holds plus the project's free slots, twice the instance's slots shared among the scans dispatching, and the setting; free slots are re-read at every refill. The nuclei passes hand the Sentinel wrapper 64 x the setting targets per call (256 by default) and write the ledger after each call. A chunk that fails or expires falls back to local execution alone, no live Sentinel (or none that claims) stops the fan-out, and a stop or global pause expires every outstanding chunk task. `CVENGINE_STAGE_PARALLEL=1` is the old serial behaviour, task for task. Applies to nmap, naabu and the nuclei technology, targeted and deep passes (internal ones too); testssl, UDP and WPScan are one host per task and unchanged. See [Pipeline](docs/PIPELINE.md#sentinel-dispatch) and [Configuration](docs/CONFIGURATION.md).
- **Mobile: a project bar and a Switch project sheet on phones and tablets (#339).** Under 1000 px a bar under the header shows the current organization and project; tapping it opens a sheet listing All projects, then each organization with its projects (the current one marked, a search box when there are more than 6, Escape to close). The last project picked in each organization is remembered, as on desktop, and a user with one project sees neither. The picker that sat in the More sheet is gone. On phones the Scans rows and the Hygiene certificates are compact cards, tab strips fade at the edge with more tabs, and the Hosts and Scans toolbars wrap cleanly at 375 px.
- **Graph details open in a slide-out drawer (#333).** Clicking a cloud, a heat bubble, a host, an address or a CVE on the dashboard's live graph or the asset graph page slides a drawer in from the right instead of pushing a card below the fold. Close, Escape, or a click on empty graph closes it; Escape still leaves an opened project once nothing is selected. The empty space under the live graph is gone (the discovery ticker takes no room until it has a line) and the "See scanner findings" link there is removed.
- **The graph's details say why, with no second click (#341).** A host's drawer lists every live CVE match and open finding behind its colour, the worst first (hotter, then exploited, then the higher score), each with its CVSS, exploited flag, AI verdict and classification, a short description and a link to the finding. A fire, smoke or ice bubble lists the 25 riskiest hosts at that heat with their reasons and a link to all of them; a hygiene bubble groups the open findings by kind with the hosts each is on; a project lists its counts and hottest hosts.
- **More of the environment-only settings are in configMaxxing: AI spend and Scan pacing.** Twelve numbers an operator wants to turn without editing `.env` and restarting can now be set in **Settings, configMaxxing**, as two new cards built from one generic registry (`cveFeed/tunables.py`: adding a tunable is one registry entry and one `tunables.value()` call where the code read the variable). **AI spend** holds the caps on paid AI calls: triage questions per run, per project per day and per day (`CVENGINE_TRIAGE_MAX_PER_RUN`, `_PER_PROJECT_DAY`, `_PER_DAY`), validation checks per task, per project per day and per day (`CVENGINE_VALIDATE_MAX_*`), the hours between two validations of an unchanged finding (`CVENGINE_VALIDATE_MIN_GAP_HOURS`) and AI template drafts per day (`CVENGINE_TEMPLATE_GEN_MAX_PER_DAY`). **Scan pacing** holds `CVENGINE_STAGE_PARALLEL` (chunks of one stage in flight across Sentinel slots, 1 to 16), `CVENGINE_WORK_MIN_GAP_HOURS`, `CVENGINE_SENTINEL_REFRESH_GAP_HOURS` and `CVENGINE_BULK_DISCOVER_NOW`. Each row shows its default, the allowed range, the environment value if one is set, where the number in effect comes from (saved here, the environment or the built-in default), who saved it and when, whether it applies live, and **Reset to default**; the same rule as Scan concurrency applies: a saved value wins over the environment, which wins over the default (the environment is not range-checked, as before). All twelve are read on every use, so a save reaches every worker within about five seconds with no restart (saved in the database, published to Redis, cached five seconds per process; the 30 second tick publishes it again so a flushed Redis heals; without Redis the environment and defaults apply as before). A setting that is only read at process start would say "Applies after the next restart"; none of these is. App Admins only (the settings API is superuser only and the change functions refuse anyone else), instance-wide rather than per project or organization, and every change is audited (`tunable.set`, `tunable.reset`, `config_maxxing.tunables_reset_all`); **Reset all configMaxxing settings to defaults** covers the new cards. Session-only settings API (`/cve/api/settings/tunables`), not part of the agent REST API or MCP. The provider, model and the planning and CVE guidance budgets stay on the AI model tab.  A Redis that restarts without persistence (or is flushed or recreated) no longer puts a saved limit back to the default: when Redis answers but holds no copy, a process reads the saved values from the database, at most once every five seconds, and publishes them again; this applies to the Scan concurrency limits too. A Redis that cannot be reached keeps the last copy a process had. The page shows the daily caps' use so far, warns (never blocks) on a cap far below its default, far above the environment value or default, or a conductor gap of 0, and an environment value the page cannot hold (a fraction of an hour, or above the range) no longer blocks saving the other boxes. `CVENGINE_STAGE_PARALLEL` is read once when a stage starts (a stage in flight keeps its number). Triage and validation count a day from UTC midnight and template drafts over the last 24 hours. An integer variable that is not a whole number (`7.9`, `1e6`) falls back to the default as before, and a number of hours in `.env` is held to ten years. Migration `cveFeed` 0132 adds one table; nothing changes until a value is saved.
- **How an AI request is made is now a setting, for every provider (settings API).** The request timeout (10 to 3600 seconds), the connect timeout (1 to 300), the reply limit (64 to 32768 tokens, for every stage), Ollama's context window (512 to 1048576 tokens), structured output (`auto`, `all`, `off`), reasoning (`default`, `off`, `low`, `medium`, `high`, `max`) and Ollama's keep-alive (`30m`, `1h`, `0`, `-1`; a duration is sent as a string, `0` and `-1` as the integers Ollama accepts for them, because it refuses the string "-1" with a 400) were environment variables in `.env` (and keep-alive was not settable at all), so changing one meant editing a file and restarting. They are now stored with the AI settings (`GET` and `PUT /cve/api/settings/ai`, App Admins only; migration `dashboard.0031`, all nullable or empty). **An empty setting follows the environment variable of the same name, then the provider's default, so an install that sets none sends the same request as before** (the Anthropic, OpenAI, Ollama, vLLM, LM Studio and OpenRouter request bodies and timeouts are unchanged, and a test compares each against the fixed body written out); **a saved value wins over the variable**, so a change in the browser is not undone by an old `.env` line. Values are checked against their range when saved (a bad one is a 400 that names the field and stores nothing, including the other fields of the request) and again when used, so a row edited by hand cannot send what a provider would refuse. The response says, per setting, the value in force and where it comes from (`effective`: setting, environment variable or default), what the environment sets (`env`), each provider's defaults (`provider_defaults`), the ranges, and which settings only apply to some providers (`context_tokens` and `keep_alive`: Ollama). A key is still write-only and still dropped when the provider or host changes; a metadata address is still refused. Each change is recorded in the audit log as `ai.settings` (the names of what changed, and the new value of the numbers and words, never a key or an address).
- **Settings, AI model has a Request settings section for those settings.** The provider list is grouped (Hosted, Your own server), and the vLLM entry now says what it is for: "vLLM, llama.cpp or another OpenAI-compatible server" (the stored value is still `vllm`). A collapsed section holds the seven request settings from the API above, each with the line "Using 600 seconds, set here", "Using 400 tokens from CVENGINE_AI_MAX_TOKENS in the server's environment" or "Using 2,048 tokens (400 for a finding triage), the default for Ollama", so a person can see what is in force and why before changing it. The context window and keep-alive appear only for Ollama, and reasoning says when a provider (vLLM, LM Studio, OpenRouter) is not sent a field. A number out of range is flagged under its box and nothing is saved until it is fixed; warnings cover a reply limit under 1,000 tokens, thinking on a limit under 4,096, a connect timeout longer than the request timeout, and a long reply (a limit of 16,384 tokens or more, or high reasoning) on a request timeout of 300 seconds or less (requests are not streamed, so one that times out is still billed). Only the boxes the chosen provider uses are checked and sent, so a leftover mistake in a hidden box cannot block a save; a refused save also shows on the page, not only in the toast; each box is tied to its hint with `aria-describedby` and the messages are announced once, when a value is committed, not on every keystroke. The Anthropic hint says older Claude models refuse a very large reply limit with a 400, and the reply limit's help describes the triage retry (twice the limit, up to 32,768). **Save and test** now leaves its result on the page (it used to vanish with the 3.5 second toast, which was too short to read a sentence of advice), and the card says when the connection was last verified. The page also follows what the server stored after a save, so switching provider back and forth restores the right address.
- **A failed AI connection test says what to change.** "Connection failed: anthropic HTTP request failed (status 401)" is now followed by a sentence by kind of failure: the key was refused, the provider is rate limiting, no answer in N seconds (N is the timeout in force, with the setting to raise), the server could not be reached (inside Docker, `localhost` is the Docker host), the address is not an AI API, the model name or path is wrong, the reply was cut off at the reply limit, or the model answered with nothing (a reasoning model that spent the limit thinking). What the provider said is never repeated. A connect timeout is told apart from a slow answer (it names the Connect timeout and the address, not the Request timeout). The test waits at most 240 seconds whatever the Request timeout is, because it runs inside a web request that the web server ends at 300 seconds, and the result says so. A triage reply cut off at a reply limit already at the 32768 ceiling is no longer asked a second, identical, paid time. A stored provider this version does not know no longer breaks the page or the test.
- **Provision a Sentinel with one command, or hand an AI agent a ready-made prompt (#354, #358).** After **Provision** the Sentinels page offers three tabs: **Command** (one line to run on the machine: it clones the Sentinel client, or reuses a clone already there, runs `setup.sh` with the blob and your choices, names the Sentinel after the machine and starts it), **AI agent** (a complete prompt with the blob, server and choices already in it, covering prerequisites, setup, confirming the Sentinel connected, removing the spent blob and what to report back) and **Blob** (the raw blob for an existing Sentinel's `.env`). The last tab you used is remembered. The server returns the two strings as `setup.one_liner` and `setup.agent_prompt`, so the MCP tool gets them too.
- **The scan detail page shows the scan as a live pipeline (#357).** One step per stage in the order they ran, joined by a line: a green check when done, a pulsing dot while running, red with its error when failed, a dashed outline while waiting. Each step shows how long it took, as text and as a bar against the longest step, the running step's timer ticks, and a summary line reads "4 of 5 stages done, now: Nuclei targeted CVE templates". Durations come from the stage records, not estimates; reduced motion turns the motion off.
- **Empty lists say what to do next (#356).** Targets, Scans, Hosts, Findings, Matches, Hygiene, Changes and Activity tell "nothing yet" apart from "nothing matches these filters" and offer the next step as a button (Add targets, Go to Targets, Clear filters, Show all scans), instead of one grey sentence.
- **A browser takes a screenshot of every web page of every host, and says why when it cannot.** The owner of an instance had never seen a screenshot of any host. The only capture was a side effect of a full bbot discovery (gowitness), so a **Rescan host**, a host found by certificate transparency, a retest or an assessment never took one; a rescan wrote a newer row without the older row's picture and hid it; gowitness could not start Chromium in the Ubuntu 26.04 image (a library name that changed with the release, a download that is x86-64 only, no `sudo`) and a hard failure of the module was never shown; it dropped every redirect, so a host that answers `301` to another host never got one; and the host page said "None yet ... rescan the host to take one", which cannot work. Now: a stage after nmap (`Web screenshots`, in every kind of run: full scans, host assessments, Rescan host, certificate transparency hosts, retests) queues the pages, and one drain on its own `screenshot_queue` (the classic worker consumes it, so the container still starts four workers) takes them with Playwright's headless Chromium, one browser and one page at a time, in a child process under the memory gate (`screenshot`, 450 MB, one at a time). Pages are every open HTTP(S) port nmap or naabu found (and the well-known web ports), at most six per host, by **host name**, following redirects; a host behind a CDN only on 80 and 443; each page in a fresh context (no cookies, downloads refused, no permissions, HTTPS errors ignored, 15 to 20 seconds). New model `HostScreenshot`, one row per project, host, scheme and port (picture and thumbnail, hash, final URL, HTTP status, title, when taken, outcome and reason; additive migrations `cveFeed` 0133 and 0134, which also schedules the backfill; tenancy scope entry). A **Take screenshot** button on the host page (`POST projects/{slug}/hosts/{id}/screenshot`, `screenshot_host`, `GET .../screenshots`, `get_host_screenshots`; the write roles, a person's request is not held back by a bug bounty project's missing confirmation but is held to its scope) and an hourly backfill (`screenshot_backfill`: hosts with no picture first, then old ones and old failures, 20 per project and 100 per run, project by project, nothing while scanning is paused or an update drains) cover the rest. **Every redirect hop and sub-resource is vetted**: the browser can connect nowhere itself, all its traffic (loopback too) goes to a loopback proxy in the worker (`cveFeed/shot_proxy.py`) that asks the exclusions, the bug bounty scope and `is_scannable_ip` after resolving, and connects to the address it vetted through Settings > Proxy when set, so a redirect to `127.0.0.1`, a metadata address or an excluded host never leaves it; the browser runs as `nobody` with a private directory and none of the worker's secrets. The host page shows each captured page with its final URL, status, title and a Compare with previous, the real reason for each page without a picture (timed out, could not be reached, TLS handshake failed, blocked: redirects to X, waiting for the worker, Chromium not installed), alt text, and the Gallery uses the same pictures. **The discovery screenshot is the fallback, and a rescan no longer hides it**: the newest same-name row that has one is shown. Pictures are served by the existing authenticated, project-checked view (by project and host name, never a path; `/media/` refuses them to non-admins), kept as the current and one earlier picture, shrunk to a JPEG above 2 MB, capped at 500 MB per project, and removed with the host. RDP: a server that insists on NLA now has an outcome ("NLA required, no pre-auth screen") on its port instead of nothing; `rdp_shot` is unchanged. `get_system_status` shows per project the hosts with a web page and a picture, what is queued, what failed and the last capture error, and for administrators the browser and its version. Image: `playwright install --with-deps --only-shell chromium` for Ubuntu 26.04 on amd64 and arm64 (about 300 MB more in the image), the Chromium build pinned and checked at build time (`PLAYWRIGHT_CHROMIUM_VERSION`, launched as root and as `nobody`), in the SBOM, THIRD-PARTY and the tool-version check (Playwright).
